%%% hover
one-fly units: The unit of every error on this page. A view's error, the root mean squared difference between the model's view and the flies' mean view, is divided by the same difference for one fly against the mean of the other flies. A value of 1 says that the model is as far from the flies as one fly is from the others, and lower is better.
megamat stage error: The root mean square, over the megamat panel's PN dendrites, PN boutons, KC claws and KC somata, of each stage's error in one-fly units. Taken on strength by odor alone, it is the number that models are chosen and gated on. Lower is better.
population error: Of one stage, panel and condition, the root mean square of the errors of its views, each in one-fly units. The views are strength by odor, sharing between odors, breadth among units and, where the units are named glomeruli, each glomerulus's response. Lower is better.
strength by odor: One of the three views of a population. Each odor's response rate (the fraction of units that respond to it) and each odor's mean response, both as multiples of their mean over the panel's odors.
sharing between odors: One of the three views of a population. For each pair of odors, the cosine distance between their responses across the units, once on which units respond and once on how strongly the responders respond.
breadth among units: One of the three views of a population. The distribution, over the units, of the number of odors that a unit responds to.
no-step yardstick: The error of taking the recordings upstream as the prediction for a stage, in that stage's one-fly units. Upstream is the stage before or, for a held-out panel's KC somata, the panel's ORN recordings. A model explains a step only so far as it is nearer the downstream recordings than that.
most atypical fly: Of the flies of one stage and panel, the fly that is furthest from the mean of the other flies, in one-fly units. A model is called inside at a stage if it is no further from the flies' mean than this fly is from the others.
best family: The model that the earlier work ended with, on eight wirings: the hemibrain's and seven re-drawn versions of it, each fitted by itself to the KC recordings of the APL16 panel and to nothing else. Its APL weights each KC by the number of odors that the KC responds to, it inhibits the PN boutons as well as the claws, and it has an inhibition among KCs. See the section on what carries over from the APL16 work.
the fitted model: The first fit of the best family to all megamat stages with the antennal lobe's normalization searched, made on half of the family's wirings against half of each stage's flies and read on the others. It is one fit. Fitted again in the other combinations of wirings and flies, its fall on megamat repeats in every one and its hold in none. See the section on the fit made again.
The fitted model: The first fit of the best family to all megamat stages with the antennal lobe's normalization searched, made on half of the family's wirings against half of each stage's flies and read on the others. It is one fit. Fitted again in the other combinations of wirings and flies, its fall on megamat repeats in every one and its hold in none. See the section on the fit made again.
re-drawn: Of the PN→KC wiring: a randomized version of the hemibrain's wiring, made either by permuting the claws among the KCs of each type or by a shuffle that keeps every glomerulus pair's count of shared KCs. Each KC keeps its number of claws and each glomerulus its number of claws. A model on a re-drawn wiring stands for another fly with the same statistics.
APL16 panel: The 16 odors of the KC recordings made with APL on and with APL off: control flies, and flies with APL output blocked (TNT). Twelve of its odors are megamat odors. The best family was fitted to these recordings, and they are held out from every fit to the megamat stages.
realism bands: Four ranges that a model run on the APL16 panel has to stay inside: its response rate with APL on, the ratio of its response rates with APL off and with APL on, its response rate ρ with APL on, and its distribution of the number of odors per KC. They are reported for every model and never optimized. See the footnote on the bands.
calibrated readout: A saturating function of a model KC's spike count n, n / (n + k), applied before the model's distances between odors are taken. The half-saturation count k is set for each model on its own spike counts with APL on, and never on the APL effect. The APL-effect statistics on this page are read through it.
baseline-noise units: A response divided by the unit's own noise before the odor, the standard deviation of its signal within the pre-odor frames. The boutons, the claws and the KC somata are counted in these units.
pattern part: The part of the APL-effect loss that is left once the model's and the flies' mean effects are removed: the root mean squared difference of the two per-pair effects about their means. It is read with its amplitude part and its shape part. Lower is better.
APL-effect loss: The root mean squared difference, over the 120 odor pairs of the APL16 panel, between the model's and the flies' per-pair APL effect: the distance between two odors with APL on minus their distance with APL off. The main target of the earlier work, now a test on recordings that a fit to the stages never sees. Lower is better.

## What is recorded: the stages, the panels and the flies {#recordings}

This page follows one target. A model of the fly's olfactory pathway is held to the responses that were recorded at every stage of that pathway: the olfactory receptor neurons (ORNs), the projection neurons (PNs) at their dendrites and at their boutons, and the Kenyon cells (KCs) of the mushroom body at their claws and at their somata. The model and the flies are compared as populations, stage by stage.

The earlier work held models to what one inhibitory neuron of the mushroom body, APL, does to the distances between odors at the KCs. It is on the page "APL16 Summary". What carries over from it is [near the end of this page](#apl16-carryover).

![The megamat recordings at every stage, the flies alone. Top row, strength by odor: each odor's response rate as a multiple of the mean over the panel's odors, the mean over the flies (dot) and the range of the flies (bar). Middle row, sharing between odors: for each pair of odors the cosine distance between the sets of units that respond, the flies' mean. Bottom row, breadth among units: the share of units that respond to a given number of odors, the flies' mean (bar) and each fly (dot). Columns: the ORNs, the PN dendrites, the PN boutons, the KC claws and the KC somata.](results/summary_stages/stage_recordings.png)

### The stages and the panels {#stages-panels}

**Five stages of one panel are recorded.** The megamat panel is {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=kc_soma:n_odors ~ .0f}} odors at one concentration, 10^-3, and it is the only panel recorded at every stage. Each stage was imaged in its own flies, all wild-type. One further fly was recorded at its claws and at its somata on the same trials.

The megamat panel, stage by stage. "Odors read" is the number of odors that every fly of the stage was given. A response is counted in units of the stage's own noise, and a unit responds to an odor if its mean over the trials is above the criterion ([how the criteria were set](#fn-criteria)).

| stage | what is imaged | a unit | flies | odors read | a response is counted in | criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORNs | the axon terminals in the antennal lobe | a glomerulus | {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=orn:n_flies ~ .0f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=orn:n_odors ~ .0f}} | the spread of single trials | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:criterion ~ .0f}} |
| PN dendrites | the dendrites in the antennal lobe, in the glomeruli that the driver line labels | a glomerulus | {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=pn_dendrite:n_flies ~ .0f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=pn_dendrite:n_odors ~ .0f}} | the spread of single trials | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:criterion ~ .0f}} |
| PN boutons | the boutons in the calyx | an imaged region, with no glomerulus label | {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=pn_bouton:n_flies ~ .0f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=pn_bouton:n_odors ~ .0f}} | the noise before the odor | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:criterion ~ .0f}} |
| KC claws | the claws in the calyx | an imaged region, not assigned to a KC | {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=kc_claw:n_flies ~ .0f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=kc_claw:n_odors ~ .0f}} | the noise before the odor | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:criterion ~ .0f}} |
| KC somata | the cell bodies | a KC | {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=kc_soma:n_flies ~ .0f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=kc_soma:n_odors ~ .0f}} | the noise before the odor | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:criterion ~ .2f}} |

**The other panels are recorded at the KC somata,** and all but the APL16 panel at the ORNs as well. They are never fitted. They say whether a fit to the megamat stages holds on odors and flies that it did not see.

| panel | recorded stages | use |
|---|---|---|
| megamat, {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=kc_soma:n_odors ~ .0f}} odors | all five stages (the table above) | fits are made to it |
| validation2, {{E137_heldout_readings/validation2_flies.csv:flies=all;rule=common_mean:n_odors ~ .0f}} odors | ORNs ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__validation2__wild_type;name=strength:n_flies ~ .0f}} flies), KC somata ({{E137_heldout_readings/validation2_flies.csv:flies=all;rule=common_mean:n_flies ~ .0f}} flies, {{E137_heldout_readings/validation2_flies.csv:flies=six;rule=e134:n_flies ~ w}} of them on {{E137_heldout_readings/validation2_flies.csv:flies=six;rule=e134:n_odors ~ .0f}} common odors) | held out; it has been read once for a regression ([caveats](#caveats)) |
| the kiwi mixture and the control mixture, each with its five components and at three dilutions | ORNs ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__kmix__wild_type;name=strength:n_flies ~ .0f}} flies), KC somata ({{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=mixtures_fitted;panel=kmix:n_flies ~ .0f}} and {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=mixtures_fitted;panel=cmix:n_flies ~ .0f}} flies) | held out; the control mixtures are in the test of whether a fit holds, and the kiwi mixtures are reported beside it, since they cannot be read on strength by odor ([caveats](#caveats)) |

**Concentrations.** Megamat is at one concentration in the recordings and in the model's input. Three odors of the APL16 panel, paa, euc and -aPine, have ORN input recorded at another concentration than the KCs were given, and every number of that panel involves them ([footnote](#fn-concentrations)).

### Three views of a population, and what the flies' views look like {#views}

**A population** is the table of trial-mean responses of all units of one stage to the odors of one panel. A bouton region has no glomerulus label, a claw region is not assigned to a KC, and a recorded KC cannot be matched to a model KC. So a model is compared with the flies on what does not depend on which unit is which.

**Three views of a population** are read, the same at every stage. They are the three rows of the figure above.

- **Strength by odor:** each odor's response rate (the fraction of units that respond to it) and each odor's mean response, both as multiples of their mean over the panel's odors.
- **Sharing between odors:** for each pair of odors, the cosine distance between their responses across the units, once on which units respond and once on how strongly the responders respond.
- **Breadth among units:** the distribution of the number of odors that a unit responds to.

Where the units are named glomeruli, at the ORNs and the PN dendrites, each glomerulus's response to each odor is read as a fourth view.

**The odors' strengths are re-ordered along the pathway.** aa is among the weakest odors at the PN dendrites, at {{E138/stages.csv:panel=megamat;condition=on;group=family_fitted;stage=pn_dendrite;row=common;odor=aa:rate_flies ~ .2f}} of the panel's mean response rate, and among the strongest at the KC somata, at {{E138/stages.csv:panel=megamat;condition=on;group=family_fitted;stage=kc_soma;row=common;odor=aa:rate_flies ~ .2f}}. B-cit goes the other way, from {{E138/stages.csv:panel=megamat;condition=on;group=family_fitted;stage=kc_claw;row=common;odor=B-cit:rate_flies ~ .2f}} at the claws to {{E138/stages.csv:panel=megamat;condition=on;group=family_fitted;stage=kc_soma;row=common;odor=B-cit:rate_flies ~ .2f}} at the somata. A model therefore cannot get the somata's strengths right by handing on the strengths of its input.

**The KC somata hold odors apart, and their cells respond to few odors.** At the ORNs nearly every glomerulus responds to many of the odors, and two odors share most of the glomeruli that respond. At the KC somata most cells respond to no odor or to one, and two odors share few of the cells that respond. The stages between lie between (the middle and bottom rows of the figure).

How much the flies' views change from one stage to the next. A value is the root mean squared change over the view's entries: the odors for strength by odor (the response-rate part, in multiples of the panel's mean) and the odor pairs for sharing between odors (on which units respond, in cosine distance). The last column counts the pairs whose change is outside zero by its 90 % interval, from resampling the flies of both stages.

| step | strength by odor,<br>change | sharing between odors,<br>change | pairs that change,<br>of the pairs read |
|---|---|---|---|
| ORNs to PN dendrites | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=orn__megamat__wild_type -> pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;part=strength/rate:rms_change ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=orn__megamat__wild_type -> pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:rms_change ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=orn__megamat__wild_type -> pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:n_interval_excludes_0 ~ .0f}} of {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=orn__megamat__wild_type -> pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:n_entries ~ .0f}} |
| PN dendrites to PN boutons | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type -> pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;part=strength/rate:rms_change ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type -> pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:rms_change ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type -> pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:n_interval_excludes_0 ~ .0f}} of {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type -> pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:n_entries ~ .0f}} |
| PN boutons to KC claws | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type -> kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;part=strength/rate:rms_change ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type -> kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:rms_change ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type -> kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:n_interval_excludes_0 ~ .0f}} of {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type -> kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:n_entries ~ .0f}} |
| KC claws to KC somata | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type -> kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;part=strength/rate:rms_change ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type -> kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:rms_change ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type -> kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:n_interval_excludes_0 ~ .0f}} of {{E134_population_baseline/flies_steps_summary.csv:step=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type -> kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;part=sharing/who:n_entries ~ .0f}} |

**Every step re-orders the strengths, and two steps remake the sharing.** The strengths change by about as much at each step after the dendrites. The sharing changes from the ORNs to the PN dendrites and most from the claws to the somata, where nearly every pair changes. Between the dendrites and the claws it changes little.

**Part of a change of that size can be a difference between sets of flies and not a step in the pathway.** Two sets of flies read at one stage, the megamat somata and the control flies' somata, differ by {{E146/sets_odors_rms.csv:part=rate:sets_diff_rms ~ .2f}} in the same units on their {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=megamat_in_control_units:n_odors ~ w}} shared odors. On those odors the step from the dendrites to the boutons changes the strengths by {{E146/sets_odors_rms.csv:part=rate:step_rms_12 ~ .2f}}. Each stage was recorded in its own flies, and the dendrites and the boutons by different experimenters ([below](#bouton-step-forms)).

## How a model is compared with the flies {#target}

### One-fly units and the floor {#one-fly-units}

**One-fly units.** A view's error is the root mean squared difference between the model's view and the flies' mean view, over the view's entries: the odors, the pairs or the bins. It is divided by the same difference for one fly against the mean of the other flies. A value of 1 says that the model is as far from the flies as one fly is from the others, and lower is better ([how it is computed](#fn-one-fly)).

**The floor** is what a resampled set of the same flies gives against the measured set. No model can be expected below it. **The most atypical fly** is the fly furthest from the mean of the others, and it sets the line for "inside" ([below](#gating)).

The flies against themselves at each megamat stage, in one-fly units. "One fly" is the mean over the flies of each fly's error against the other flies, with the least and the most atypical fly in parentheses.

| | strength by odor {span=2} | sharing between odors {span=2} | breadth among units {span=2} |
| stage | floor | one fly | floor | one fly | floor | one fly |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORNs | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_max ~ .2f}}) | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_max ~ .2f}}) | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_max ~ .2f}}) |
| PN dendrites | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_max ~ .2f}}) | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_max ~ .2f}}) | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_dendrite__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_max ~ .2f}}) |
| PN boutons | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_max ~ .2f}}) | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_max ~ .2f}}) | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_max ~ .2f}}) |
| KC claws | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_max ~ .2f}}) | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_max ~ .2f}}) | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_claw__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_max ~ .2f}}) |
| KC somata | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:loo_max ~ .2f}}) | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=sharing:loo_max ~ .2f}}) | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:floor ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_mean ~ .2f}} ({{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=kc_soma__megamat__wild_type;name=breadth:loo_max ~ .2f}}) |

**The room for a model is between the floor and one fly.** By construction one fly reads about 1. The floors are near a third at every stage, so a model at 1 has about two thirds of a one-fly unit left to gain.

### Like for like {#like-for-like}

Both sides are read the same way at every stage, and a stage's reading is fixed before any model is read on it.

- **The flies' responses** are counted in units of their own noise, with one response criterion per stage ([footnote](#fn-criteria)).
- **The model's units are sampled and pooled as the recording was made:** the glomeruli that the driver line labels, and {{json:results/E134_population_baseline/lock.json:readings.constants.CLAW_REGION_SIZE}} claws to a claw region.
- **The model's values pass through one monotone curve per stage,** set on the pooled values and never on anything about odors. An indicator's unknown curve is then neither counted as error nor used to hide one ([footnote](#fn-stage-curve)).
- **The model is given the flies' trial noise.**

Every stage has a second reading as well, with another curve, another pooling or raw spike counts. A stage whose verdict changes between the two is "not readable" and stays out of the comparison until a reading holds.

### The numbers that are compared {#numbers}

- **The population error** of a stage is the root mean square of its views' errors, each in one-fly units: one number per stage, panel and condition.
- **The megamat stage error** is the root mean square of the errors of four megamat stages: the PN dendrites, the PN boutons, the KC claws and the KC somata. The ORN stage is the model's input, so it is read and not counted.
- **The megamat stage error on strength by odor** takes each stage's strength error alone. It is the number that models are chosen and gated on, for the reason of the next subsection.

### Why strength by odor alone chooses and gates {#measure}

**A measure can choose between models only if it orders them alike against different flies.** That was tested before any model was fitted to a stage. Saved model variants inside the realism bands were scored at the KC somata of the APL16 panel against two random halves of the recordings. A measure was to pass if the two halves agree at {{json:results/E134_population_baseline/lock.json:rules.gate.B_reading.min_consistency}} or more with APL on and with APL off.

How alike two halves of the recordings order the saved models. A cell is the ρ, over {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=on;measure=error;variant_set=inside_bands:n_variants ~ .0f}} saved variants, between a measure's values against one half and against the other, the mean over {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=on;measure=error;variant_set=inside_bands:n_splits ~ .0f}} random splits. A value of 1 says that both halves put the variants in the same order.

| statistic | APL on | APL off |
|---|---|---|
| the population error over the three views | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=on;measure=error;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=off;measure=error;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} |
| strength by odor | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=on;measure=view_strength;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=off;measure=view_strength;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} |
| sharing between odors | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=on;measure=view_sharing;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=off;measure=view_sharing;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} |
| breadth among units | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=on;measure=view_breadth;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=off;measure=view_breadth;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} |
| strength and sharing together | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=on;measure=error_strength_sharing;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=off;measure=error_strength_sharing;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} |
| ρ on odor-odor distances | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=on;measure=dist_rho;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=off;measure=dist_rho;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} |
| response rate ρ | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=on;measure=rate_rho;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} | {{E134_population_baseline/archive_consistency_curve_noise.csv:version=half;condition=off;measure=rate_rho;variant_set=inside_bands:split_half_rho ~ .2f}} |

**Strength by odor passes, and nothing else built from the views does.** The error over the three views is just under the line with APL on and well under it with APL off. Sharing between odors fails with APL off, and breadth fails in both conditions. Strength and sharing together fall short with APL off. So strength by odor alone chooses and gates models.

**No model is chosen by a correlation.** ρ on odor-odor distances clears the line and response rate ρ does not. Neither sees a view's level or its spread, so neither is a candidate. Both are still reported.

**Why breadth cannot order the models.** Once a model's values pass through the stage's curve, its response rate, and with it most of its breadth, is the flies'. What is left differs less between the variants than a resampled set of flies moves: the middle half of the variants' breadth errors spans {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_breadth.csv:reading=curve_noise;condition=on;measure=breadth:iqr ~ .2f}} with APL on, against a floor of {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_breadth.csv:reading=curve_noise;condition=on;measure=breadth:flies_floor ~ .2f}}.

**What sharing and breadth add beside it.** They are reported for every model at every stage, with the error over all three views. They say where a model is furthest from the flies, as leads. And they guard a gain: a change that lowers the strength error and raises another view's error by more than that error's spread over the wirings is not an improvement.

**At the megamat stages the test was made later, on saved settings.** Over {{json:results/E143_megamat_fly_sampling/splits.json:n_settings ~ .0f}} settings and every split of each stage's flies, the megamat stage error on strength by odor orders the settings alike: its consistency is {{json:results/E143_megamat_fly_sampling/splits.json:stages.megamat_stage_error.median ~ .2f}} at the median split and {{json:results/E143_megamat_fly_sampling/splits.json:stages.megamat_stage_error.min ~ .2f}} at the least. No single stage does, and the claws and the somata least ([below](#fly-sampling)).

### Choosing and gating {#gating}

- **An improvement** lowers the megamat stage error on strength by odor, on wirings and flies that the change was not fitted to, by more than the wirings' spread. It raises no stage's error and no held-out panel's by more than that stage's own spread.
- **A model is read as a population of flies.** The best family exists on eight wirings, each read as one fly, and a model's value is the mean over its wirings. Fits use four wirings and half of each stage's flies, and are read on the four other wirings against the other half.
- **A stage is "inside"** if the model is no further from the flies' mean than the most atypical fly is from the other flies, on {{const:experiments/E134_population_baseline.py:INSIDE_MIN_WIRINGS ~ w}} of the eight wirings. It is "far" if it is above {{const:experiments/E134_population_baseline.py:FAR_ERROR ~ .0f}} on as many.
- **A view's, a stage's or a single odor's share of an error is a lead,** never a result. Near-ties are confirmed on held-out flies, wirings or panels.
- **Realism is a constraint, not a target.** The four realism bands hold for every model run on the APL16 panel ([footnote](#fn-realism-bands)), and every section names what would be hard to defend in a fly.

## Where the best models stand on the stage target {#best-models}

**The model.** Its stages are the fly's: ORNs, PNs with the antennal lobe's normalization, PN boutons, KC claws and KC somata, on the wiring of one mapped brain, the hemibrain. One APL neuron is driven by the KCs and inhibits the claws and the boutons. With APL off its output weights are zero and nothing else changes.

**The baseline is the best family.** It is the model that the earlier work ended with, on the hemibrain's wiring and on seven re-drawn ones. Each wiring was fitted by itself to the KC recordings of the APL16 panel and to nothing else ([what carries over](#apl16-carryover)).

**One form of the model has been fitted to the megamat stages, and its first fit is nearer the flies.** It is the family with the antennal lobe's normalization searched, from the best family fitted to all megamat stages at once ([below](#stage-fit)). On wirings and flies that its fit did not see, its megamat stage error is lower than the unrefitted family's by {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=fall:value ~ .3f}}, against a bar of {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=bar:value ~ .3f}}. It is in the last column of the best-models table. That first fit is "the fitted model" on this page.

**Its three changes have been read apart.** The weaker normalization alone gives most of the gain on megamat, made before the somata, and moves the somata of validation2 and of the APL16 panel with APL on away from the flies. Only with both of its calyx scales does that fit hold, and every setting that improves raises the pattern part of the APL-effect loss ([below](#normalization-ladder)).

**What its hold on the held-out panels rests on has been read as well.** The hemibrain's matrix of the inhibition among KCs keeps those panels from {{E141/writeup_values.csv:arm=D1.8_inhadd1000;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=kckc_factor:value ~ .1f}} to {{E141/writeup_values.csv:arm=D5.8_inhadd1000;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=kckc_factor:value ~ .2f}} times the family's scale, and no dose does more than the fitted model's. No other form holds in its place: not a pooled neuron, a gain at the claws, a rise with APL on only, or the matrix shuffled within pairs of KC types. So the fitted model rests on the hemibrain's counts of synapses between KCs, read as an inhibition and scaled up ([below](#kckc-rise)).

**How far these verdicts depend on the recorded flies has been read as well.** With the flies resampled, the fitted model's fall on megamat stands: {{E143_megamat_fly_sampling/intervals_changes.csv:table=E136_stage_population_fit;arm=A1;statistic=megamat:all_flies ~ +.3f}} against all the flies, and {{E143_megamat_fly_sampling/intervals_changes.csv:table=E136_stage_population_fit;arm=A1;statistic=megamat:lo ~ +.3f}} to {{E143_megamat_fly_sampling/intervals_changes.csv:table=E136_stage_population_fit;arm=A1;statistic=megamat:hi ~ +.3f}} over the draws. Its hold on the held-out panels is read on {{E143_megamat_fly_sampling/class_shares.csv:table=E136_stage_population_fit;arm=A1:share_scored ~ .2f}} of the draws, and the holds at the two ends of the range of doses on {{E143_megamat_fly_sampling/class_shares.csv:table=E141_kckc_rise_forms;arm=D1.8_inhadd1000:share_scored ~ .2f}} and {{E143_megamat_fly_sampling/class_shares.csv:table=E141_kckc_rise_forms;arm=D5.8_inhadd1000:share_scored ~ .2f}}: all three are near-ties ([below](#fly-sampling)).

**A PN stage nearer the flies' dendrites does not help the fitted model.** With the recorded dendrites arranged on the fitted model's own PN rates, the dendrites come nearer the flies, and the boutons and the claws move away, by {{json:results/E142_dendrite_bouton_step/step1_classes.json:classes.dendrites_carry.stages.pn_bouton.change ~ +.3f}} and {{json:results/E142_dendrite_bouton_step/step1_classes.json:classes.dendrites_carry.stages.kc_claw.change ~ +.3f}}. So the largest stage error left, at the boutons, is not made before the dendrites. The flies re-order the odors from the dendrites to the boutons, and the model's step does not ([below](#dendrite-bouton-step)). No weight per recorded glomerulus and no feature of the whole odor makes that step on odors that it was not fitted to, and the step is not told apart from what two sets of recordings differ by at one stage ([below](#bouton-step-forms)).

**On a second mapped brain the fall repeats and the hold does not.** The fitted model's three changes were set on the male CNS's two calyces, each with its own wiring, APL synapses and matrix among KCs. The megamat stage error changes by {{E144/writeup_values.csv:variant=L_A1;stage=stage;statistic=change:value ~ +.3f}} and {{E144/writeup_values.csv:variant=R_A1;stage=stage;statistic=change:value ~ +.3f}} there, about as much as on the hemibrain. The changes hold on neither calyx, and on the hemibrain's own wiring read alone they are megamat only as well. On the male CNS the family itself is further from the flies from the boutons on ([below](#male-calyces)).

**Fitted again on the other wirings and against the other halves of the flies, the fall repeats and the hold does not.** The same form was fitted in the {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/classes.json:len(experiment.cells_scored) ~ w}} other combinations of a half of the family's wirings with a half of each stage's flies. Where each is read, the megamat stage error changes by {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=stage;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=read;statistic=change:value ~ +.3f}}, {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=stage;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=read;statistic=change:value ~ +.3f}} and {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=stage;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=read;statistic=change:value ~ +.3f}}, a fall beyond its bar and beyond the control fit's fall every time. None of the new fits holds: each loses a megamat stage, another view or a held-out panel, and one ends at a bound of its search. Of the fitted factors only the rise of the inhibition among KCs comes back in every fit, at no one dose ([below](#stage-fit-again)).

**So the fitted model is one fit of a form.** The form's gain before the calyx is found every time, and its hold was found once. What the sections below say about the fitted model's hold, its parts and what it rests on is said of that one fit.

**The megamat stage error orders the saved models alike on every split of the flies,** through the stages before the calyx. Megamat's claws and somata do not order models that differ only in the calyx, so forms of the calyx are judged on the held-out panels. The fits made again bear this out: they agree on a weaker antennal-lobe normalization and end at different calyces ([below](#stage-fit-again)).

**Two operating points are read.** "As fitted" keeps each wiring's parameters from its fit. "Held" sets the response rate with APL on to that of the panel's flies and keeps the wiring's own ratio of the response rates with APL off and with APL on. On megamat the flies respond at {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_operating_points.csv:model=C2;panel=megamat;reading=fitted:flies_rate_on ~ .4f}} and the family as fitted at {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_operating_points.csv:model=C2;panel=megamat;reading=fitted:rate_on_min ~ .3f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_operating_points.csv:model=C2;panel=megamat;reading=fitted:rate_on_max ~ .3f}}.

### The stage table: the best family at every megamat stage {#stage-table}

Each megamat stage on strength by odor, in one-fly units: 1 says that the model is as far from the flies' mean as one fly is from the other flies, and lower is better. A value of the family is the mean over its eight wirings, run end to end from the ORN input. The last two columns give the population error over all three views and its class.

| | the flies, strength by odor {span=2} | no step | the best family, strength by odor {span=3} | the best family, all three views {span=2} |
| megamat stage | floor | the most atypical fly | the recordings upstream<br>taken as the prediction | as fitted<br>(lowest to highest wiring) | held at the flies'<br>response rate | class | as fitted | class |

![Strength by odor at each megamat stage and as the megamat stage error, in one-fly units: the flies' floor (dark bar), the most atypical fly (orange bar), the recordings upstream taken as the prediction (square), the best family held at the flies' response rate on each wiring (small dots) and as the wirings' mean (large dot), and the family with another PN stage put in: the recorded dendrites imposed, the global stage and the stage with glomerulus terms (triangles). Lower is better.](results/summary_stages/stage_strength_by_model.png)

**On strength by odor the best family is inside the flies' spread at every megamat stage,** on all eight wirings, as fitted and held. From the PN dendrites to the KC somata it is about as far from the flies as one fly is from the others. The line is loose: "inside" is measured against the most atypical fly, not against the floor. What a fit can still gain is the distance between one fly and the floor.

**The ORN row is the reading itself.** There the model's side is the model's input, the lab's own ORN recordings, passed through the same curve, sampling and noise as a model's stage. Its value is what the reading alone costs.

**The model's first two steps add nothing on strength by odor.** Its PN dendrites are further from the flies than the ORN recordings taken as the prediction, and its boutons are level with the dendrite recordings taken so. Only in the calyx does the model come nearer the flies than the stage before. The dendrites' yardstick is read on the {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_steps.csv:group=megamat_fitted;step=orn->pn_dendrite:n_odors ~ .0f}} odors given to all the ORN flies, on which the model's dendrites read {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_steps.csv:group=megamat_fitted;step=orn->pn_dendrite:model_strength ~ .2f}}.

**At the boutons that yardstick is of the size of a difference between two sets of flies.** The dendrite recordings taken as the prediction for the boutons read {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=no_step:measured ~ .2f}}, and {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=no_step:lo ~ .2f}} to {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=no_step:hi ~ .2f}} over resampled flies. Two sets of flies read at one stage, the megamat somata and the control flies' somata, are {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=megamat_in_control_units:measured ~ .2f}} and {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=control_in_megamat_units:measured ~ .2f}} apart in each other's one-fly units ([below](#bouton-step-forms)).

**On all three views two stages are not readable.** The boutons and the claws are inside, and no stage is far. The PN dendrites sit at the line, and the somata's class differs between the reading through the curve and raw spike counts.

**Where the family is furthest from the flies** is sharing between odors at the dendrites ({{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=pn_dendrite:default_view_sharing_mean ~ .2f}}) and at the somata ({{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=kc_soma:default_view_sharing_mean ~ .2f}} as fitted, {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_held;stage=kc_soma:default_view_sharing_mean ~ .2f}} held), and breadth at the dendrites ({{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=pn_dendrite:default_view_breadth_mean ~ .2f}}). These are leads.

### The best family and the variants read at every stage {#best-models-table}

The models that have been read at every megamat stage, on eight wirings each and against all the flies but for the last two columns. The megamat and held-out rows are errors in one-fly units, lower is better, and a value is the mean over the wirings. The three variants keep the family's calyx and put another PN stage in, with both response rates held again ([below](#antennal-lobe-dendrites)). The held-out mixtures are read as uploaded, with the flies' trial noise on the model.

**The last two columns are of a fit, and are read where it was not fitted.** The model is the family with the antennal lobe's normalization searched ([below](#stage-fit)). Its megamat and held-out rows are means over the {{json:results/E136_stage_population_fit/measure_strength/classes.json:len(classes.A1.megamat.changes) ~ w}} wirings that its fit did not use, megamat against the half of each stage's flies that the fit did not see. It is the first of {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/classes.json:len(cells) ~ w}} fits of that form. The others are read on other wirings and against other flies, each beside its own reading of the unrefitted family, so they stand in their own section and not in this table ([below](#stage-fit-again)).

**Those two columns are compared with each other only.** Against half of the flies every model reads higher, so the unrefitted family read the same way stands beside the fitted model. In the realism and APL-effect rows the fitted model is read on all eight wirings, to be compared with the family as fitted.

The realism rows and the APL-effect rows are of the APL16 panel, where the family is as fitted: the model with APL on against the control flies, and with APL off against APL output blocked (TNT). The APL-effect statistics are read through the calibrated readout, each wiring scored by itself ([how they are defined](#fn-apl-effect)).

| ρ on the per-pair APL effect | ceiling {{data:apl_effect_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}} | {{E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/writeup_values.csv:arm=family;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=apl_effect;statistic=cal_apl_effect_spearman_mean:value ~ .2f}} | — | {{E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/writeup_values.csv:arm=imposed;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=apl_effect;statistic=cal_apl_effect_spearman_mean:value ~ .2f}} | {{E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/writeup_values.csv:arm=R1;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=apl_effect;statistic=cal_apl_effect_spearman_mean:value ~ .2f}} | {{E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/writeup_values.csv:arm=rung;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=apl_effect;statistic=cal_apl_effect_spearman_mean:value ~ .2f}} | — | {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=apl_effect;half=all;statistic=apl_effect_rho_mean:value ~ .2f}} |
| Pearson r on the per-pair APL effect | ceiling {{data:apl_effect_pearson_ceiling ~ .2f}} | {{E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/writeup_values.csv:arm=family;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=apl_effect;statistic=cal_apl_effect_pearson_mean:value ~ .2f}} | — | {{E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/writeup_values.csv:arm=imposed;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=apl_effect;statistic=cal_apl_effect_pearson_mean:value ~ .2f}} | {{E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/writeup_values.csv:arm=R1;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=apl_effect;statistic=cal_apl_effect_pearson_mean:value ~ .2f}} | {{E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/writeup_values.csv:arm=rung;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=apl_effect;statistic=cal_apl_effect_pearson_mean:value ~ .2f}} | — | {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=apl_effect;half=all;statistic=apl_effect_pearson_mean:value ~ .2f}} |

**One variant is nearer the flies than the family: the one fitted to the megamat stages.** With the antennal lobe's normalization searched, the megamat stage error is {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=stage_error_mean:value ~ .3f}} against the unrefitted family's {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=family;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=stage_error_mean:value ~ .3f}}, on wirings and flies that the fit did not see. Every megamat stage is lower, the PN dendrites and the PN boutons most, and the KC somata by less than the family's spread over its wirings ([the fit](#stage-fit)). The same form fitted again on the other wirings and against the other halves of the flies comes nearer the flies on megamat every time ([below](#stage-fit-again)).

**The three variants with another PN stage put in are not.** Each brings a stage before the calyx nearer the flies: the dendrites with the recorded dendrites imposed, the boutons with a fitted stage. Each moves the claws and the somata away, and the megamat stage error rises under each.

**The held-out panels stand where megamat does, or cannot be read.** On strength by odor the validation2 somata and the control mixtures' somata are inside. The kiwi mixtures have two values and no verdict: the family is far against the components' first block and inside against their second ([the held-out panels read like for like](#heldout-readings)).

**The fitted model's gain is on megamat alone.** On validation2, on the control mixtures as uploaded and on the APL16 panel its somata stay within the family's spread over its wirings, neither nearer the flies nor further. That is as scored. With the flies resampled, the APL16 panel's somata with APL on are worse than that spread on {{E143_megamat_fly_sampling/intervals_changes.csv:table=E136_stage_population_fit;arm=A1;statistic=panel/apl16/apl16/on:share_worse ~ .2f}} of the draws and the control mixtures' on {{E143_megamat_fly_sampling/intervals_changes.csv:table=E136_stage_population_fit;arm=A1;statistic=panel/mixtures/cmix/on:share_worse ~ .2f}} ([below](#fly-sampling)). Against the kiwi components' second block it is further from the flies than the family by more than that spread, as it is on the control mixtures under the same reading ([the fit](#stage-fit)).

**Realism.** Every model of the table is inside the four realism bands on the APL16 panel, and none is flagged. Holding the family at the megamat flies' response rate is a large move, though: the KC threshold falls to {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_operating_points.csv:model=C2;panel=megamat;reading=held:thr_factor_min ~ .2f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_operating_points.csv:model=C2;panel=megamat;reading=held:thr_factor_max ~ .2f}} of each wiring's fitted value, and the largest spike count of a KC reaches {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_operating_points.csv:model=C2;panel=megamat;reading=held:max_count_vs_fitted_max ~ .2f}} times the fitted run's.

**The fitted model holds two parameters that would be hard to defend.** The additive term of its PN normalization is {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=factor;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=fit_S;statistic=inhadd:value ~ .1f}} times the family's, which leaves the antennal lobe's normalization much weaker. Its scale of the inhibition among KCs is {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=factor;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=fit_S;statistic=kckc:value ~ .2f}} times the family's. Its largest spike count of a KC with APL on is {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=realism;half=all;statistic=max_count_on_min:value ~ .0f}} to {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=realism;half=all;statistic=max_count_on_max:value ~ .0f}} over the wirings, against the family's {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=family;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=realism;half=all;statistic=max_count_on_min:value ~ .0f}} to {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=family;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=realism;half=all;statistic=max_count_on_max:value ~ .0f}}.

**The APL-effect rows are a test on recordings that no stage fit sees.** The family was fitted to them, so its own column there is a fit. For the variants the rows say what a change made for the stages costs: every change of the antennal lobe raises the APL-effect loss, in the shape part of its pattern, and lowers both correlations. That holds for the fitted model as well: it comes nearer the flies on the megamat stages and moves away from them on the APL effect.

**The fit with a firing ceiling is level with the family, and the bouton baseline is worse.** The ceiling, from the firing ceiling for the KCs in the earlier work, leaves the megamat stage error where the family has it ([what carries over](#apl16-carryover)). The bouton baseline, the model before any of the earlier work's mechanisms, differs from the family at the somata alone.

**On megamat and on the APL16 panel the family and the fit with a firing ceiling are the nearest models.** Their fits to the APL16 recordings brought their somata nearer the flies on both panels.

**On validation2 they are not.** The lab's published hemibrain model, which the family's fit does not inform, reads lower there than the family, and it stays at or below the family when all of validation2's flies and odors are read ([the held-out panels read like for like](#heldout-readings)). So on strength by odor the fit to the APL16 panel is not shown to carry over to validation2.

## How far the best model has come on the stage target {#stage-progress}

The plot follows the best model so far on each statistic of the stage target, against the time at which each experiment was completed. A point is one experiment's best model on that statistic, and a staircase steps down where a model sets a new best. Errors are in one-fly units and lower is better, with lines at 1, where a model is as far from the flies as one fly is from the others, and at the flies' floor.

**The series.** The first panel holds the megamat stage error on strength by odor, the strength error of each megamat stage, and the KC somata's strength error on validation2, on the control mixtures as uploaded and on the APL16 panel with APL on and with APL off. A second panel holds the APL-effect loss and its pattern part. Those two are descriptions of a held-out test, and no model is chosen by them.

**A fitted model is drawn by its change where it was not fitted.** Every line starts at the best family's value on its eight wirings against all the flies. A fitted model is drawn at that value plus its change from the family on the wirings and flies that its fit did not see. Its own readings there are in its popup: against half of the flies every model reads higher, off the scale of the lines.

**Open points take no step.** A staircase steps only on the family's own readings and on a model that holds: one whose megamat stage error falls beyond its bar with no stage, view or held-out panel worse than the family's spread. A point is open if its model has another class or none: megamat only, a fall at another stage's or another view's cost, no fall beyond the bar, a fit that ended at a bound of its search. It is open if its value holds a stage that the model was fitted to or set from, on flies not held out of that fit: the PN dendrites of the models of the antennal lobe held to the recorded PN dendrites, and a megamat stage error that includes them. And it is open on another brain's wiring: a setting on a male calyx is drawn from that calyx's own family, and a step is a change of the model and not of the brain ([below](#male-calyces)).

%%% html results/summary_progress/stage_progress_plot.html

**The megamat stage error has taken one step that clears its bar.** It starts at {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_error.csv:group=megamat_held;wiring=model:stage_error_strength ~ .3f}}, the family held at the megamat flies' response rate, and falls to {{diff:E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_error.csv:group=megamat_held;wiring=model:stage_error_strength -- E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=fall:value ~ .3f}} with the antennal lobe's normalization searched, in the best family fitted to all megamat stages at once ([below](#stage-fit)). The lines of the megamat stages step with it, the PN dendrites and the PN boutons furthest. On the held-out panels the fitted model stays within the family's spread over its wirings, so a small step there is not a gain. Two doses of the inhibition among KCs that hold beside the fitted model's set further bests, at the megamat stage error as well, each by less than the family's spread: near-ties, not gains ([below](#kckc-rise)). In the second panel the fitted model's APL-effect loss lies above the family's.

**That step is one fit's.** The same form fitted again on the other wirings and against the other halves of the flies lowers the megamat stage error every time and holds in none, so none of those fits takes a step ([below](#stage-fit-again)). Their points are open. On the megamat stage error the lowest of them lies just above the fitted model's, and at the PN dendrites and the PN boutons below it.

## What the stage experiments found {#stage-experiments}

### Before the calyx the model is too even across odors {#before-calyx}

**Five experiments of the earlier work read the model's stages before the somata** against the megamat recordings: the model's stages against the flies' stages; the model's boutons, claws and claw-to-soma step against the per-ROI recordings; the bouton stage held to the recorded boutons; an expansive output at the PN boutons; and the flies' bouton totals by tuning group. Their verdicts on the APL effect are in the APL16 Summary. One finding bears on the stage target.

**The model evens out the odors' total drive before the calyx, and the flies do not.** The spread of the odors' totals is the coefficient of variation, over twelve megamat odors, of the response summed over the units. In the model it falls from the ORN input to the PNs. In the flies' PN dendrites and boutons it is several times the model's.

The spread of the odors' totals, stage by stage. The flies' ORNs and PN dendrites and the model's input and PNs are read on the {{json:results/E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/lock.json:len(glomeruli)}} glomeruli that all of them hold, on the hemibrain's wiring. A flies' cell gives the value and its 90 % interval, or for the boutons the range of the five flies.

| stage | the flies | the best family | the family at its<br>weakest normalization | the family with<br>a fixed gain |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ORNs (the model's input) | {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=ORNs, megamat recordings (pebbled);source=flies, 28 shared glomeruli, linear:value ~ .3f}} ({{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=ORNs, megamat recordings (pebbled);source=flies, 28 shared glomeruli, linear:lo ~ .3f}} to {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=ORNs, megamat recordings (pebbled);source=flies, 28 shared glomeruli, linear:hi ~ .3f}}) | {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=ORN input;source=model input, 28 shared glomeruli:value ~ .3f}} | {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=ORN input;source=model input, 28 shared glomeruli:value ~ .3f}} | {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=ORN input;source=model input, 28 shared glomeruli:value ~ .3f}} |
| PNs (in the flies, the PN dendrites) | {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=PN dendrites (GH146);source=flies, 28 shared glomeruli, linear:value ~ .3f}} ({{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=PN dendrites (GH146);source=flies, 28 shared glomeruli, linear:lo ~ .3f}} to {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=PN dendrites (GH146);source=flies, 28 shared glomeruli, linear:hi ~ .3f}}) | {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=PN stage;source=model, E102 step identity (28 shared glomeruli):value ~ .3f}} | {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=PN stage;source=model, E102 step inhadd10000 (28 shared glomeruli):value ~ .3f}} | {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=PN stage;source=model, E102 step gain_fixed (28 shared glomeruli):value ~ .3f}} |
| PN boutons, APL on | {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=boutons;source=flies (5):value ~ .3f}} ({{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=boutons;source=flies (5):lo ~ .3f}} to {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=boutons;source=flies (5):hi ~ .3f}}) | {{E120/a5_bound.csv:stage=boutons;source=model default, APL on, rate (8 wirings):value ~ .3f}} | — | — |
| PN boutons, APL off (mean of the eight wirings) | — | {{E120/a2_summary.csv:step=identity;reading=rate;criterion=2.0:totals_cv ~ .3f}} | {{E120/a2_summary.csv:step=inhadd10000;reading=rate;criterion=2.0:totals_cv ~ .3f}} | {{E120/a2_summary.csv:step=gain_fixed;reading=rate;criterion=2.0:totals_cv ~ .3f}} |

**No change of the PN stage or of the bouton closes it.** Four candidates were read, and none brings the boutons' spread inside the flies' range on any wiring: a calcium indicator that expands strong responses, a weaker normalization in the antennal lobe (the steps of the antennal lobe's normalization at the strength the flies' APL asks for), another dose of APL's inhibition at the boutons, and a bouton output raised to a power of up to 3, which reaches {{E127/step0_bouton_summary.csv:q=3.0;factor=1.0;cond=on;reading=rate;criterion=2.0:totals_cv ~ .3f}} against the lowest fly's {{E119/a2_spread.csv:criterion=2.0:totals_cv_lo ~ .3f}}. The model's input is itself more even than the flies' boutons, and its PN stage can only even it out.

**The weakest normalization costs the APL effect.** Fitted again there, the family's pattern part of the APL-effect loss rises by {{diff:E102/d_compare_inhadd10000.csv:unit=S;row=refit at inhadd10000:cal_z8_pattern -- E102/d_compare_inhadd10000.csv:unit=S;row=unchanged fits:cal_z8_pattern ~ +.4f}} (worse).

**Part of the flies' value is how their regions were sampled.** With every group of like-tuned bouton regions counted once, the five flies read {{min:E128/a_readings.csv:*:equal ~ .2f}} to {{max:E128/a_readings.csv:*:equal ~ .2f}}, against {{min:E128/a_readings.csv:*:measured ~ .2f}} to {{max:E128/a_readings.csv:*:measured ~ .2f}} as measured. The model's boutons stay a quarter to a half as uneven as the flies' however the regions are counted.

**At the claws no reading of the model is the flies'.** The model's strong responses are too weak beside the rest, at the boutons and at the claws. In the one fly recorded at claws and somata on the same trials, the somata's response rates follow the claws' summed response from odor to odor. In the model that summed drive is nearly the same for every odor.

**What the stage target makes of it.** The even PN stage shows in the stage table: the model's dendrites and boutons are no nearer the flies than the recordings before them. Putting another PN stage into the unchanged calyx does not help the later stages ([below](#antennal-lobe-dendrites)). A weaker normalization of the model's own PN stage does, as far as the claws, with the calyx fitted along ([below](#stage-fit)) or left as it is ([below](#normalization-ladder)). Only with the calyx fitted along do the held-out panels stay where the family has them, and only in the first of the fits made of it ([below](#stage-fit-again)). The recorded dendrites put under that fitted calyx do not help the later stages either ([below](#dendrite-bouton-step)).

**The flies' step from the dendrites to the boutons has no form on the recorded glomeruli either.** No weighting of those glomeruli and no feature of the whole odor makes it on odors that it was not fitted to ([below](#bouton-step-forms)).

### The baseline: one error per stage {#population-baseline}

**The experiment is the models as populations at every recorded stage.** It changed no model. It fixed every stage's reading before any model was read, scored the flies alone, scored {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_tradeoff.csv:reading=curve_noise;measure=error_on:n_variants ~ .0f}} saved model variants at the KC somata of the APL16 panel, and ran the family and five other models on the megamat and the held-out inputs. Its two main results are the measure ([above](#measure)) and the stage table ([above](#stage-table)).

**The steps.** On the population error over all views the model is nearer the flies than the no-step yardstick at every step: by little at the boutons ({{E134_population_baseline/writeup_steps.csv:group=megamat_fitted;step=pn_dendrite->pn_bouton:model_downstream_error ~ .2f}} against {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_steps.csv:group=megamat_fitted;step=pn_dendrite->pn_bouton:no_step_error ~ .2f}}) and by much at the somata ({{E134_population_baseline/writeup_steps.csv:group=megamat_fitted;step=kc_claw->kc_soma:model_downstream_error ~ .2f}} against {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_steps.csv:group=megamat_fitted;step=kc_claw->kc_soma:no_step_error ~ .2f}}). On strength by odor it is not at the first two steps.

**The population view and the APL effect agree only loosely.** Over the saved variants, the population error with APL on follows the pattern part of the APL-effect loss at a ρ of {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_tradeoff.csv:reading=curve_noise;measure=error_on:rho_with_cal_pattern_part ~ .2f}}, and strength by odor at {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_tradeoff.csv:reading=curve_noise;measure=strength_on:rho_with_cal_pattern_part ~ .2f}}. The models nearest the flies on the APL effect are not the ones nearest them as populations. This is a description, and no variant is chosen by it.

### The held-out panels read like for like {#heldout-readings}

**The question was whether the baseline's verdicts on the held-out panels are the model's or the reading's.** The baseline read the mixture flies from one uploaded value per cell and stimulus, and validation2 on part of its flies and odors. In the held-out panels read like for like, the family's saved runs are scored against the flies read in several ways. No model was run.

**The readings.** A mixture session has a fixed order: the components, then the mixtures, then in most flies the components again ([caveats](#caveats)). The mixture flies were read as uploaded and by position in the session. Validation2 was read on the baseline's flies and on all its flies and odors, under two rules for flies given part of the panel.

![The best family's error on strength by odor at the KC somata of each mixture panel under each reading of the flies, with the flies' trial noise on the model: each wiring (small dots), the wirings' mean (large dot), a flat profile (diamond), the ORN recordings taken as the prediction (square), the most atypical fly (orange bar), the flies' floor (dark bar) and the line above which a model is far (dotted). The components' first block is the one before the mixtures, and their second block the one after.](results/E137_heldout_readings/strength_by_reading.png)

**The kiwi mixtures cannot be read on strength by odor.** The family is far from the flies when the components are read before the mixtures, and inside when they are read after them. Every other model moves the same way, and so do the ORN recordings taken as the prediction. So the panel cannot say whether a fit generalizes.

**The control mixtures and validation2 hold.** The family is inside on both under every reading, on all eight wirings. On validation2 it is not nearer the flies than a flat profile under two of the three readings, and the rule for partial panels moves it more than the added flies do.

**One read needs no component: the mixture's rise over its dilutions.** From the weakest to the strongest dilution the response rate rises {{E137_heldout_readings/rise_summary.csv:panel=kmix;reading=components_first_block;units=noise;side=flies;group=flies:rate_0_over_m2 ~ .2f}} times in the kiwi flies and {{E137_heldout_readings/rise_summary.csv:panel=kmix;reading=raw_counts;units=spikes;side=model;group=fitted:rate_0_over_m2 ~ .2f}} times in the family, and {{E137_heldout_readings/rise_summary.csv:panel=cmix;reading=components_first_block;units=noise;side=flies;group=flies:rate_0_over_m2 ~ .2f}} against {{E137_heldout_readings/rise_summary.csv:panel=cmix;reading=raw_counts;units=spikes;side=model;group=fitted:rate_0_over_m2 ~ .2f}} times on the control panel. There the family agrees with the flies.

**The baseline's sharing errors at the mixtures were mostly the units.** In each cell's z-score over its session the family's sharing error read {{E137_heldout_readings/rescore_scores.csv:panel=kmix;reading=as_uploaded;units=z;noise=False;group=fitted;wiring=mean:view_sharing ~ .2f}} and {{E137_heldout_readings/rescore_scores.csv:panel=cmix;reading=as_uploaded;units=z;noise=False;group=fitted;wiring=mean:view_sharing ~ .2f}} on the two panels, and in baseline-noise units {{E137_heldout_readings/summary_strength.csv:panel=kmix;reading=components_first_block:family_sharing ~ .2f}} and {{E137_heldout_readings/summary_strength.csv:panel=cmix;reading=components_first_block:family_sharing ~ .2f}}.

### The antennal lobe held to the recorded PN dendrites {#antennal-lobe-dendrites}

**The question was whether the megamat stage error is made before the calyx.** In the antennal lobe held to the recorded PN dendrites, the model's PN stage is held to the recorded dendrites in two ways. It is fitted to them, and the recorded dendrites themselves are put in its place ("imposed"). The family's calyx stays as it is, with both response rates held again.

**With the calyx unchanged, none of these PN stages carries to the claws or the somata.** With the recorded dendrites imposed, the dendrites come nearer the flies, and the boutons, the claws and the somata all move away, on every wiring. The fitted stages bring the boutons nearer and move the claws and the somata further away. With the family's calyx as it is, each of these PN stages is a worse input to it. A weaker normalization of the model's own stage does carry as far as the claws on megamat, with the calyx fitted along ([below](#stage-fit)) or left as it is ([below](#normalization-ladder)).

**"Imposed" keeps the flies' arrangement and the model's rates.** On the {{json:results/E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/lock.json:len(glomeruli)}} glomeruli that the recordings and the model's input share, each PN's rate is set from the flies' mean dendrite response, rank for rank on the model's own rates. The dendrites enter in units of the flies' own noise, and once more as the change in fluorescence over its baseline (dF/F).

Strength by odor at the four megamat stages with each PN stage put into the family, in one-fly units; lower is better. A value is the mean over the eight wirings, held at the megamat flies' response rate.

| PN stage put into the family | PN dendrites | PN boutons | KC claws | KC somata | megamat stage error<br>(lowest to highest wiring) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

**The recorded arrangement is better than its permutations.** With the glomerulus labels or the odor labels permuted, every stage after the dendrites is further from the flies than with the recorded arrangement, so the runs read the dendrites' structure. The verdict is the same in dF/F, and with the somata read on raw spike counts. The class fixed beforehand is "not upstream": on none of the eight wirings, where {{const:experiments/E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites.py:MIN_WIRINGS ~ w}} were asked, do the imposed dendrites lower the somata's error and halve the boutons'.

The PN stage alone on flies and odors that its fit did not see, in one-fly units. A stage is fitted to one half of the PN flies and part of the odors, and read on the other half and the odors left out. A cell gives the two directions: from the first half to the second / from the second to the first.

| PN stage | each glomerulus's response<br>to each odor | strength by odor |
