| the other wirings | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_inhadd:value ~ .0f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=rise_inhadd:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_aplpn:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=rise_aplpn:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_kckc:value ~ .2f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=rise_kckc:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_inhsc:value ~ .2f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=start_loss:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_loss:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=both;statistic=consistency_rho:value ~ .2f}} |

**Of the fitted factors only the rise of the inhibition among KCs comes back in every fit, and as a direction.** That scale is determined in every fit. It ends at {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=b;statistic=best_kckc:value ~ .2f}}, {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_kckc:value ~ .2f}} and {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_kckc:value ~ .2f}} times the family's, and with both exchanged at {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/E145_lock.json:fit.bounds.kckc.1 ~ .1f}}, the top of the range that the search allowed. A fit that stops at the top of its range gives that value or more, not a value. So the megamat stages ask for a rise among KCs on every split, and they fix no dose.

**The factors before the calyx do not come back in every fit.** The additive term stays inside the range fixed beforehand against the other flies and on the other wirings, and ends above it with both exchanged. The bouton scale is determined with both exchanged and on the other wirings. Against the other flies it is not: its return costs less than the line, and that fit is read at the family's bouton scale. The scale of the PN normalization, which was not read for this, ends anywhere from {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=b;statistic=best_inhsc:value ~ .2f}} to {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_inhsc:value ~ .2f}} times the family's.

**The bound decides none of the classes.** The fit with both exchanged has no class by the rule for a bound. Its fall is made before the calyx, so "the fall repeats" stands with it counted. Read without that rule it would be one stage at another's cost, as the fit against the other flies is: its megamat stage error falls beyond its bar and its claws are worse than their spread. Its bouton scale was found beside the bound and is read with it, and its additive term does not turn on it. No wider search follows from the bound.

**The loss of the hold follows neither the flies nor the wirings alone.** The fit against the other flies shares the first fit's wirings, and the fit on the other wirings shares its flies. Both differ from the first fit in class. That is what megamat's claws and somata led one to expect: they do not order models that differ only in the calyx, so a fit against other flies or on other wirings ends at another calyx ([above](#fly-sampling)).

**On the other wirings the two halves of the flies order the fit's evaluations differently.** Over a fit's evaluations, the megamat stage errors against the fitted half follow those against the other half at a ρ of {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=both;statistic=consistency_rho:value ~ .2f}} with both exchanged and {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=both;statistic=consistency_rho:value ~ .2f}} against the other flies, near the first fit's {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=both;statistic=consistency_rho:value ~ .2f}}. On the other wirings the ρ is {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=both;statistic=consistency_rho:value ~ .2f}}: the half that the fit did not see was lowest early in the search, and the fitted half went on improving without it. This is a description, as for the first fit ([above](#stage-fit)).

**With the flies resampled the verdict stands, and the classes of the fits against the other flies and on the other wirings are near-ties.** "The fall repeats" is read on {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/e143_intervals.json:derived.fall_repeats.share_scored ~ .2f}} of the draws, and on no draw does a new fit hold. The class of the fit against the other flies is read on {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=stage;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=read;statistic=resampled_share_scored:value ~ .2f}} of the draws, and megamat only on {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/e143_intervals.json:class_shares.F.shares.megamat only ~ .2f}}. That of the fit on the other wirings is read on {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=stage;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=read;statistic=resampled_share_scored:value ~ .2f}}, and one stage at another's cost on {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/e143_intervals.json:class_shares.W.shares.one stage at another's cost ~ .2f}}.

The APL effect on the APL16 panel for the fits, a test on recordings that no fit of the stages saw: the mean over the eight wirings, read through the calibrated readout against all the recordings ([how the statistics are defined](#fn-apl-effect)). The family was fitted to these recordings, so its row is a fit. Lower is better for the loss and its pattern part. The rise of the pattern part is from the family's on the wirings that the fit did not use, the largest of its readings against all the recordings and against each half of them.

| model | APL-effect loss | mean APL effect | pattern part | rise of the<br>pattern part | ρ on the per-pair<br>APL effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the flies: floor, value or ceiling | floor {{data:apl_effect_rmse_floor ~ .3f}} | {{data:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}} | floor {{data:apl_effect_pattern_floor ~ .3f}} | — | ceiling {{data:apl_effect_rho_ceiling ~ .2f}} |
| the family, not refitted | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=family;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_loss:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=family;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_mean:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=family;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_pattern:value ~ .3f}} | — | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=family;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_rho:value ~ .2f}} |
| the first fit | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_loss:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_mean:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_pattern:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=price_value:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_rho:value ~ .2f}} |
| both exchanged | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_loss:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_mean:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_pattern:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=price_value:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_rho:value ~ .2f}} |
| the other flies | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_loss:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_mean:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_pattern:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=price_value:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_rho:value ~ .2f}} |
| the other wirings | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_loss:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_mean:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_pattern:value ~ .3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=price_value:value ~ +.3f}} | {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_rho:value ~ .2f}} |

**Every fit pays a price on the APL effect.** The pattern part rises in every fit by more than the {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/classes.json:cells.X.price.threshold ~ .3f}} that counts as a price, and most with both exchanged. The mean APL effect moves much less, so the price is in the pattern across the pairs. ρ on the per-pair APL effect falls in every fit, to {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=apl_effect;panel=apl16;condition=on_off;half=all;statistic=cal_rho:value ~ .2f}} with both exchanged. These rows describe a held-out test, and no fit is chosen by them.

**Realism,** on the APL16 panel, which no fit saw, over all eight wirings. Both response rates are held in every read.

- **No wiring of any fit leaves a realism band.** The fit on the other wirings comes nearest: its response rate ρ with APL on is {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=realism;half=all;statistic=rate_rho_on_min:value ~ .2f}} on its lowest wiring, against the band's {{const:kcq/apl_effect.py:RATE_RHO_MIN ~ .2f}} and the family's {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=family;stage=kc_soma;panel=apl16;condition=realism;half=all;statistic=rate_rho_on_min:value ~ .2f}}.
- **With both exchanged the scale of the inhibition among KCs would be hard to defend.** At {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/E145_lock.json:fit.bounds.kckc.1 ~ .1f}} times the family's it is above the largest dose read along the ladder, {{E141/writeup_values.csv:arm=D5.8_inhadd1000;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=kckc_factor:value ~ .2f}} ([above](#kckc-rise)), for a term with no named carrier. On megamat the KCs' threshold falls to {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=reads;panel=megamat;condition=scales;half=all;statistic=fixed_thr_min:value ~ .1f}} to {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=reads;panel=megamat;condition=scales;half=all;statistic=fixed_thr_max:value ~ .1f}} over the wirings, against the first fit's {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=reads;panel=megamat;condition=scales;half=all;statistic=fixed_thr_min:value ~ .1f}} to {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=reads;panel=megamat;condition=scales;half=all;statistic=fixed_thr_max:value ~ .1f}}. A KC's largest spike count with APL on reaches {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=reads;panel=megamat;condition=scales;half=all;statistic=max_count_on_max:value ~ .0f}} there, against {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=reads;panel=megamat;condition=scales;half=all;statistic=max_count_on_max:value ~ .0f}}.
- **The additive term is as large as the first fit's or larger in every new fit.** It ends at {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_inhadd:value ~ .0f}} on the other wirings, {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=b;statistic=best_inhadd:value ~ .0f}} against the other flies and {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=b;statistic=best_inhadd:value ~ .0f}} with both exchanged, against the family's {{E139/writeup_values.csv:arm=pn_port_family;stage=pn_dendrite;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=pn_inhadd:value ~ .1f}}. The KCs at a low concentration, which respond less under a weaker normalization ([above](#normalization-ladder)), were not read here.
- **Where APL acts differs from fit to fit.** On megamat the share of the drive that APL takes from the claws that is taken at the PN boutons is {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=calyx;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=all;statistic=bouton_share_mean:value ~ .2f}} in the first fit, {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=calyx;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=all;statistic=bouton_share_mean:value ~ .2f}} with both exchanged and {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=calyx;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=all;statistic=bouton_share_mean:value ~ .2f}} on the other wirings. Against the other flies, where the family's bouton scale is kept, it is {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=calyx;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=all;statistic=bouton_share_mean:value ~ .2f}}. The connectome puts {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=connectome;stage=calyx;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=all;statistic=bouton_share:value ~ .3f}} of APL's calyx synapses there.
- **The cost of the falls is not in the bands.** It is in the stages, views and panels that each new fit loses, and in the price on the APL effect.

**Limits.**

- **The control fit's fall is that of the first fit's experiment,** where the control searched the calyx scales alone ([above](#stage-fit)). A control with all of the fit's factors searched had not been read when the fits were scored. A larger control fall could only take falls away. The fit against the other flies clears the present one by least: a fall of {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/classes.json:cells.F.cls.fall ~ .3f}} against {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/classes.json:control_fall ~ .3f}}.
- **The shares of the draws are approximate for the fits with both exchanged and against the other flies.** For them each model is read once against all the flies. At the megamat stage error that reading differs from the score's own by {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=X;stage=stage;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=read;statistic=shortcut_max_diff:value ~ .3f}} and {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=stage;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=read;statistic=shortcut_max_diff:value ~ .3f}}, beyond the tolerance of {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/E145_lock.json:resampled.read_once_check ~ .2f}} set beforehand.
- **The wirings on which earlier fits were read have been searched here.** The fits with both exchanged and on the other wirings search the wirings that every earlier fit kept for reading. Each is read on the other wirings, so no reading here or above is of a wiring that its own fit searched. That exchange was made for these fits alone, and a later fit does not search those wirings unless that is decided.
- **The pattern part was not split.** For the new fits its amplitude part and its shape part, the model's spread of the effect and Pearson r were not read.
- **The flies are few after the dendrites,** and a fit is read on half of the family's wirings, as for the first fit ([above](#stage-fit)).
- **Validation2 is held out here.** Nothing was chosen from the regression that was read on its odors ([caveats](#caveats)).
- **Concentrations.** Every number of the APL16 panel involves paa, euc and -aPine ([footnote](#fn-concentrations)).

**What follows.**

- **The gain before the calyx is the form's.** A weaker normalization of the antennal lobe lowers the megamat stage error in every fit, on wirings and flies that the fit did not see, at the PN dendrites and the PN boutons.
- **The fitted model's hold is one split's.** No other fit of its form holds. The fitted model stays the one fit that holds as scored, and it is not a model found twice.
- **The megamat stages give the calyx a direction and no values.** Every fit asks for a rise of the inhibition among KCs, and the other calyx factors change from fit to fit. Statements above about what the fitted model's hold rests on are statements about one fit.
- **Nothing follows by itself.** No wider search is made on the bound. A fit on all the flies or on all the wirings, a fit under another loss and a fit from another base each wait for the word of those who direct the project ([pending](#pending)).

## What carries over from the APL16 work {#apl16-carryover}

The work before this target is on the page "APL16 Summary". It held models to the per-pair APL effect: for each pair of odors of the APL16 panel, the distance between their KC responses with APL on minus the distance with APL off. Only what bears on reading the stage target is repeated here.

**What the best family is.** It is the model of [the stage table](#best-models), with three mechanisms that the earlier work added.

- **An APL that weights KCs by their breadth:** a KC's weight from APL grows with how broadly the KC responds without APL.
- **APL's inhibition of the PN boutons,** beside its inhibition of the claws.
- **An inhibition among KCs,** weighted by the hemibrain's synapse counts between them, that stays when APL's output is blocked.

**How it was fitted.** Four parameters were fitted to the per-pair APL effect of the APL16 recordings, with both response rates held at the flies' ([the APL-effect fits](#fn-apl-effect)). The fit was made on the hemibrain's wiring and again on seven re-drawn wirings, each by itself. No megamat stage, and no recording before the somata, entered a fit.

**Where it stood on the earlier target.** As a population of eight wirings, its pattern part of the APL-effect loss is {{E72/population.csv:group=all:cal_pattern ~ .3f}} against a floor of {{data:apl_effect_pattern_floor ~ .3f}}, and its mean APL effect is {{E72/population.csv:group=all:cal_mean ~ +.3f}} against the flies' {{data:apl_effect_mean ~ +.3f}}. Its ρ on odor-odor distances is {{E72/population.csv:group=all:raw_rho_on ~ .2f}} with APL on and {{E72/population.csv:group=all:raw_rho_off ~ .2f}} with APL off, against ceilings of {{data:ctrl_ceiling ~ .2f}} and {{data:tnt_ceiling ~ .2f}}.

**On other panels it does not beat its own input on the distances between odors.** Against the megamat KC recordings its ρ on odor-odor distances is {{E115/a_two_sets.csv:reader=family;remy=corr;condition=on:rho_remy ~ .3f}}, and the ORN input alone gives {{E115/a_two_sets.csv:reader=input orn_pearson;remy=corr;condition=on:rho_remy ~ .3f}} (the megamat gap against two sets of flies and the yardstick of the price in response rate ρ). On validation2 the two values are {{E107/c_rhos.csv:panel=validation2;reader=family;reading=fitted;member=pop;remy=table_cos;set=136;subset=all:rho ~ .3f}} and {{E107/a_rhos.csv:panel=validation2;reader=orn;remy=table_cos;set=136;subset=all:rho ~ .3f}}, against a ceiling of {{E107/a_ceilings.csv:panel=validation2;metric=cosine;set=136;subset=all:ceiling ~ .3f}} (the four validation2 odors in the panel their ORN input was recorded in).

**Three of its choices would be hard to defend in a fly.** The family as it stands keeps all three, and the best family fitted to all megamat stages at once bears on each ([above](#stage-fit)). On the APL16 panel all eight wirings are inside the four realism bands.

- **Where its APL acts** (the PN boutons' share of APL's inhibition). {{min:E94/a1_path.csv:wiring!=mean;wiring!=common:bouton_share ~ .2f}} to {{max:E94/a1_path.csv:wiring!=mean;wiring!=common:bouton_share ~ .2f}} of the drive that its APL takes from the claws is taken at the PN boutons, where the connectome puts a fraction of {{E94/a2_connectome.csv:set=calyx, all:share_pn ~ .2f}} to {{E94/a2_connectome.csv:set=calyx, assigned, KCs with a type:share_pn ~ .2f}} of APL's calyx synapses. Refitted to the megamat stages, the share falls to {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A0;stage=calyx;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=bouton_share_mean:value ~ .2f}}, and that move by itself gains no more than a control fit.
- **Its inhibition among KCs has no named carrier in the calyx.** The family needs it (KC-KC inhibition held at zero in the best family): fitted again without it, its pattern part rises from {{E110_no_kckc/readings.csv:kind=family;unit=all8;readout=cal:all_pattern ~ .3f}} to {{E110_no_kckc/readings.csv:kind=B;unit=all8;readout=cal:all_pattern ~ .3f}} (worse). On the stage target the family is nearer the flies with it as well: without it the megamat somata's strength error rises by {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A4;stage=kc_soma;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=change:value ~ +.3f}}. The fitted model needs it at a larger scale and as the hemibrain's own matrix: neither a pooled neuron nor the matrix shuffled within pairs of KC types holds in its place, and which feature of the matrix the hold needs is not separated ([above](#kckc-rise)).
- **Its PN stage is too even across odors** ([above](#before-calyx)). A weaker normalization of the antennal lobe is the one change that brings the megamat stages nearer the flies in a fit by more than a control fit gains. In the first such fit it holds, and only together with two changed scales of the calyx, one of them a larger scale of the inhibition among KCs ([above](#normalization-ladder)). With the flies resampled its fall on megamat stands and its hold is a near-tie ([above](#fly-sampling)). On the male CNS's two calyces, and fitted again on the hemibrain's other wirings and against the other halves of the flies, the fall repeats and the hold does not ([above](#male-calyces), [above](#stage-fit-again)).

**Its APL is now a held-out test.** The APL-effect statistics are still computed for every model run with APL on and off, and they keep their rows in the best-models table. They choose and gate nothing. A ranking on them is not a ranking on the stages: the two agree only loosely ([the baseline](#population-baseline)).

## Caveats {#caveats}

**The mixture sessions have a fixed order, so the kiwi mixtures gate nothing.** In a mixture session the components are given first, the mixtures after them, and in most flies the components once more.

- **Every presentation of an odor evokes less than the one before.** The components' second block evokes {{min:E137_heldout_readings/position_decline.csv:panel=kmix;units=noise:component_rate_later_over_first_block ~ .2f}} to {{max:E137_heldout_readings/position_decline.csv:panel=kmix;units=noise:component_rate_later_over_first_block ~ .2f}} of their first block's response rate in the kiwi flies (the median per fly).
- **The fall follows an odor's own repeats, not the time in the session.** In the megamat sessions, whose order is drawn anew for each block, an odor's response rate falls by a factor of {{min:E137_heldout_readings/component_fall/megamat_repeats.csv:fall_with=repeats:rate_factor_per_repeat ~ .2f}} to {{max:E137_heldout_readings/component_fall/megamat_repeats.csv:fall_with=repeats:rate_factor_per_repeat ~ .2f}} per repeat in three of the four flies, where as many trials of other odors cost a factor of {{min:E137_heldout_readings/component_fall/megamat_repeats.csv:fall_with=repeats:rate_factor_per_block_of_places ~ .2f}} to {{max:E137_heldout_readings/component_fall/megamat_repeats.csv:fall_with=repeats:rate_factor_per_block_of_places ~ .2f}}.
- **So no reading sets fresh mixtures against fresh components.** The mixtures come after trials of their own components, while the components' first block is fresh, and the model has no adaptation. Sessions with the mixtures first or interleaved would settle it, and they would be new recordings.

![Each component's single-trial response rate over its first presentation's, for its six presentations: three in the block before the mixtures and three in the block after them (the median over the flies with a second block). The components given right after the mixtures, EtOH in the kiwi sessions and ms in the control sessions, are coloured.](results/E137_heldout_readings/component_fall/fall_presentations.png)

**What was decided for the mixture panels.**

- **The kiwi mixtures leave the test of whether a fit holds.** They are reported beside it, with the readings against the components' first block and against their second block as bounds. No fit is gated on them.
- **The control mixtures stay in the test,** read as uploaded, with the second-block reading beside. The family is inside on them under every reading, so their class does not turn on the choice.
- **Megamat and validation2 are not touched.** Their order is drawn anew for each block and fly, so the fall enters the flies' spread and not their mean. The order of the APL16 recordings is not in their processed tables.

**Validation2 has been read once, for a regression.** The test of a common cause behind the odors' strength errors was judged on validation2's odors ([above](#strength-entries)). No class of glomeruli passed, so nothing is built on its result. Still, validation2 is no longer an unseen panel for anything chosen from those classes or from those readings: a change proposed from them has to be checked on other held-out odors or flies. For fits that take nothing from that test, validation2 stays held out.

**Further limits of the readings.**

- **The flies are few after the dendrites:** {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=pn_bouton:n_flies ~ w}} bouton flies, {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=kc_claw:n_flies ~ w}} claw flies and {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=kc_soma:n_flies ~ w}} soma flies, so a half of a stage's flies is two flies. The megamat soma flies respond at two levels, from {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_soma_criteria.csv:cohort=megamat, Remy's four flies:rate_min ~ .3f}} to {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_soma_criteria.csv:cohort=megamat, Remy's four flies:rate_max ~ .3f}}, which shows on sharing and breadth and not on strength by odor.
- **"Inside" is a loose line.** It is measured against the most atypical fly, and a model that is inside can still be most of a one-fly unit above the floor.
- **The measure's consistency rests on few flies after the boutons.** Among saved variants it was first shown at one stage of one panel, the KC somata of the APL16 panel. At the megamat stages it has since been read over {{json:results/E143_megamat_fly_sampling/splits.json:n_settings ~ .0f}} saved settings, on every split of each stage's flies ([above](#fly-sampling)). The megamat stage error passes as a whole, carried by the dendrites and the boutons. The claws and the somata, with two flies to a half, do not order the settings alike, and least the settings that differ only in the calyx.
- **The equal weights are a convention.** The megamat stage error weighs its four stages alike, and a population error its views. Every stage's and every view's error is given beside a combined number for that reason. Whether the boutons keep their equal weight is an open decision ([pending](#pending)).
- **The same held-out panels have judged many settings.** The fits of the megamat stages, the settings along the normalization and the doses and forms of the inhibition among KCs were all classed on the somata of the same panels. A class by a narrow margin is then a near-tie, to be confirmed on other held-out flies, wirings or panels. Read with the flies resampled, the fitted model's hold and the holds of the inhibition among KCs at {{E141/writeup_values.csv:arm=D1.8_inhadd1000;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=kckc_factor:value ~ .1f}} and at {{E141/writeup_values.csv:arm=D5.8_inhadd1000;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=kckc_factor:value ~ .2f}} times the family's scale are all near-ties ([above](#fly-sampling)).
- **The fitted model is one fit.** It was made on one half of the wirings against one half of each stage's flies. The same form fitted in the other combinations falls on megamat every time and holds in none, and its calyx factors change from fit to fit ([above](#stage-fit-again)). So a statement about what the fitted model's hold rests on is a statement about that one fit.
- **The stages are different flies, indicators and compartments.** The PN-dendrite recordings hold {{json:results/E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/lock.json:len(glomeruli)}} of the model's {{json:results/E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/timing_step1.json:size.n_input}} input glomeruli, and the bouton and claw regions carry no labels. The dendrites and the stages after them were recorded by different experimenters as well, and the flies' step from the dendrites to the boutons is not told apart from what two sets of flies differ by at one stage ([above](#bouton-step-forms)).
- **The family was fitted to the APL16 panel.** That panel is held out from fits to the megamat stages, and not for the family as it stands. Twelve of its odors are megamat odors.
- **One mapped brain carries the family.** Seven of the family's eight wirings are shuffles of the hemibrain's, and the range over the eight is not the range between flies. On the male CNS's two calyces the family as fitted there is above that range at megamat's boutons, claws and somata ([above](#male-calyces)).
- **Concentrations.** Every number of the APL16 panel involves paa, euc and -aPine ([footnote](#fn-concentrations)).

## Experiments running or not yet run, and what each would tell us {#pending}

Pending work is judged on the megamat stage error on strength by odor, on wirings and flies that a change was not fitted to, with every stage's error and the other views beside it. The held-out panels say whether a gain is general, and what a change does to the realism bands and to the APL-effect loss is reported with it.

- **Whether a further fit of the megamat stages is made, and under which loss (a decision for those who direct the project).** The form of the fitted model has been fitted in all {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/classes.json:len(cells) ~ w}} combinations of a half of the wirings with a half of each stage's flies. The megamat stage error falls in every fit, and the first fit alone holds ([above](#stage-fit-again)).
  - **What is settled.** The weaker antennal-lobe normalization is a gain before the calyx on every split. The fitted model's hold is one split's, so it is not a model found twice.
  - **What is not.** The calyx factors change from fit to fit. The megamat stages give a direction, a rise of the inhibition among KCs, and no values, so these fits choose no point to build on.
  - **What a further fit would be.** A fit on all the flies or on all the wirings, a fit under another loss, or a fit from another base. None is made without the word of those who direct the project.
  - **A lead for another loss.** Each new fit lowered its loss and lost a megamat stage, another view or a held-out panel where it was read. A loss that also holds the claws' and the somata's other views, the guard of the classes made a part of the loss, might keep a hold from split to split. That is not known: megamat's claws and somata do not order models that differ only in the calyx ([above](#fly-sampling)).
  - **A lead at the claws.** With both exchanged and against the other flies the gain at the PN dendrites comes with claws further from the flies. In the first fit and on the other wirings it does not. What differs at the claws between them has not been read.
  - **The bound of one fit is not searched further.** The fit with both exchanged ended at the top of the range allowed to the scale of the inhibition among KCs. That decides none of its readings, so no wider search and no refit follow. It stands as evidence under the entry on how the calyx is constrained.
  - **A control fit with all of the fit's factors searched (running when the fits were scored).** The control whose fall the classes use searched the calyx scales alone. The same control with every factor of the fit searched, on flies with permuted odor labels, gates nothing. A fall larger than the present control's could only take falls away, and the smallest fall of a new fit is {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/classes.json:cells.F.cls.fall ~ .3f}} against the present {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/classes.json:control_fall ~ .3f}}.
- **The megamat stage error read on model flies with each ORN fly's own input (running).** Every bar and spread since the fit of the megamat stages is taken over the family's eight wirings at one averaged ORN input. The megamat stage error through the flies' sampling gave every class an interval over the stage's flies ([above](#fly-sampling)). The flies behind the model's input are the source of variation that is left unread.
  - **What it reads.** The unrefitted family and the fitted model are run with the input of each of the {{json:results/E147_megamat_input_flies/lock.json:len(input_flies.flies) ~ w}} megamat ORN flies in place of their mean, and each such model fly is read as a wiring is read. Nothing is fitted or searched, and no class as scored changes.
  - **If the averaged input is a fair stand-in and the input fly adds little spread,** the stage table and the classes stand as read on the model's side.
  - **If the input fly moves a model as far as its wiring does,** the spreads that the classes use are a part of what model flies vary by. The classes that turn on those spreads by a narrow margin would then be read beside the wider spread. That is a reading for those who direct the project, and the classes stand as scored until their word.
  - **If the population of model flies reads a megamat stage differently from the averaged input,** the megamat stage error differs between two readings of the model. Which of them is the stage's reading is a decision for those who direct the project.
  - **If the fitted model's fall does not stand through the input flies,** that goes to them at once, beside the fits made again ([above](#stage-fit-again)).
- **How the calyx is to be constrained (a decision for those who direct the project).** Megamat's claws and somata, {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=kc_soma:n_flies ~ w}} flies each, do not order models that differ only in the calyx. So a fit of the megamat stages chooses the stages before the calyx, and little after them ([above](#fly-sampling)).
  - **What the fits made again add.** Fitted in {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/classes.json:len(cells) ~ w}} combinations of wirings and flies, the megamat stages end at a different calyx each time. The bouton scale ends anywhere from {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_aplpn:value ~ .3f}} times the family's to the family's own, and the scale of the inhibition among KCs anywhere from {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=b;statistic=best_kckc:value ~ .2f}} times the family's to the top of the range that the search allowed ([above](#stage-fit-again)). That one fit stopped at that bound is evidence of the same kind: megamat's stages did not stop the rise inside the range. No wider search is made on it.
  - **More megamat flies after the boutons.** Older megamat soma recordings exist and are held back at present. Whether more flies would be enough is not given. On the APL16 panel with APL off and on validation2 more flies to a half order the settings more alike. On the APL16 panel with APL on, five recordings to a half do no better than two.
  - **A panel with many flies in the fit.** The APL16 panel or validation2 would constrain the somata, and would then no longer be a held-out panel (the APL16 recordings are the subject of an entry below).
  - **aa's step settled first.** Without aa's entry megamat's somata nearly tell the saved settings apart. A reading of the step from the claws to the somata that settles aa may then do as much as more flies.
  - **Until one is chosen,** forms of the calyx are judged on the held-out panels, and megamat's changes at the claws and the somata are given as descriptions.
- **Whether to build on the fitted model's inhibition among KCs, and at which dose (a decision for those who direct the project).** Read along its dose and in other forms, only the hemibrain's matrix itself keeps the held-out panels, at about {{E141/writeup_values.csv:arm=D1.8_inhadd1000;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=kckc_factor:value ~ .1f}} to {{E141/writeup_values.csv:arm=D5.8_inhadd1000;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=kckc_factor:value ~ .2f}} times the family's scale ([above](#kckc-rise)). No pooled neuron, gain at the claws, rise with APL on only or shuffle of the matrix does, so the hold of the fitted model, one fit, rests on the hemibrain's counts of synapses between KCs. Megamat does not choose a dose. Along it validation2's somata come nearer the flies and the APL effect moves away, so the dose is a choice between two held-out readings.
  - **What the fits made again add.** Every fit of the fitted model's form ends with a rise of that inhibition: at {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=F;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=b;statistic=best_kckc:value ~ .2f}}, {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=W;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_kckc:value ~ .2f}} and {{E145/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=fit;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=a;statistic=best_kckc:value ~ .2f}} times the family's scale, and with both halves exchanged at the top of the search's range, {{json:results/E145_stage_fit_replication/E145_lock.json:fit.bounds.kckc.1 ~ .1f}} ([above](#stage-fit-again)). So the megamat stages ask for a rise on every split and choose no dose, and no fit but the first holds at the dose that it ends at. With the flies resampled every hold along the dose is a near-tie, the fitted model's among them ([above](#fly-sampling)).
  - **What a second brain adds.** On the male CNS's two calyces, each with its own matrix, the fitted model's factor holds on neither. One dose holds on the right calyx, {{json:results/E144_a1_malecns_calyces/E144_lock.json:definitions.settings.D5.8.kckc ~ .2f}} times its fitted scale, on {{E144/e143_class_shares.csv:arm=R_D5.8:share_scored ~ .2f}} of the draws of the flies, and on the hemibrain's own wiring read alone no dose holds ([above](#male-calyces)). So a dose that holds is not one number across calyces. A hold at any dose would be confirmed on other held-out flies or wirings before it is built on.
  - **The edges of the range (open only if the choice of dose comes to rest on them).** With the flies resampled, the hold at {{E141/writeup_values.csv:arm=D1.8_inhadd1000;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=kckc_factor:value ~ .1f}} times the family's scale is read on {{E143_megamat_fly_sampling/class_shares.csv:table=E141_kckc_rise_forms;arm=D1.8_inhadd1000:share_scored ~ .2f}} of the draws and the hold at {{E141/writeup_values.csv:arm=D5.8_inhadd1000;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=kckc_factor:value ~ .2f}} times on {{E143_megamat_fly_sampling/class_shares.csv:table=E141_kckc_rise_forms;arm=D5.8_inhadd1000:share_scored ~ .2f}} ([above](#fly-sampling)). So the recorded flies place neither edge, and a read at a dose below the lower one would not either. Other held-out flies or panels could. If a hold stands on them, the range starts at that dose. If none does, no dose is shown to hold.
  - **A carrier with a name (a lead, not yet a proposal).** MB-C1's two cells, neurons of the calyx with synapses to and from the KC claws in the hemibrain, were the one named carrier among the planned forms. They were to be read only if a pooled neuron stood in, and none did. If a form built on them keeps the held-out panels, the rise has a carrier. If it does not, the fitted model stays on a stand-in.
- **What the hemibrain's matrix of the inhibition among KCs has that the male ones lack (a lead, not yet a proposal).** On the male CNS's two calyces the fitted model's three changes lower the megamat stage error and hold on neither. The calyces' own matrices keep what the shuffle removes from the hemibrain's, and they differ in density and in the mix of KC types ([above](#male-calyces)). The earlier work moved the differences between the two brains one at a time, the matrix's shares by pair of KC types among them, and read the APL effect only (the differences between the hemibrain and the male CNS's left calyx moved one at a time).
  - **What it would read.** The same moves under the stage target: a male calyx given the density of the hemibrain's matrix or its shares by pair of KC types, and the hemibrain given a male calyx's, at the fitted model's factor and at the doses beside it. Each would be classed as the fit was, against the calyx's own family on four wirings, with the flies resampled beside.
  - **If a moved feature carries a hold into a male calyx,** the fitted model's hold rests on that feature of the matrix, and it is what a carrier of the inhibition would have to have.
  - **If none does,** the hold rests on the calyx around the matrix: its claws, its APL synapses or the scales that the family was fitted to there.
  - **What limits it.** A form of the calyx is judged at the somata of the held-out panels, and megamat's claws and somata do not order such forms ([above](#fly-sampling)). On the right calyx the classes turn on which flies are drawn, so a hold or a miss by a narrow margin would be a near-tie. And the hold that such a move would carry is one fit's: fitted again on the hemibrain's own wirings, it did not repeat ([above](#stage-fit-again)).
- **How much the PN boutons weigh in the megamat stage error, and what a stage recorded in other flies is held to (two decisions for those who direct the project).** The boutons are the megamat stage at which the fitted model stays furthest from the flies: {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=pn_bouton;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=strength_mean:value ~ .3f}} against {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=yardstick_half_to_half;stage=pn_bouton;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=strength:value ~ .3f}} for what the fitted half of the flies predicts for the other half ([above](#stage-fit)). From the dendrites to the boutons the flies re-order the odors, and no saved model's step does ([above](#dendrite-bouton-step)).
  - **What has closed.** The lead of a form at that step. The connectomes' counts of boutons, the way the flies' regions are counted, APL's inhibition of the boutons at the fit's scale and a PN stage nearer the recorded dendrites were ruled out as its source. Neither one weight per recorded glomerulus nor a feature of the whole odor makes the step on odors that it was not fitted to. And the step is not told apart in size from what two sets of flies differ by at one stage ([above](#bouton-step-forms)).
  - **The first decision: the boutons' weight.** The megamat stage error weighs its four stages alike, by convention. On wirings and flies that its fit did not see, the fitted model's is {{E136/measure_strength/writeup_values.csv:arm=A1;stage=four_stages;panel=megamat;condition=on;half=held;statistic=stage_error_mean:value ~ .3f}}. With the boutons at what the fitted half of the flies predicts for the other half it would be {{json:results/E142_dendrite_bouton_step/lock.json:probe.room.boutons_at_half ~ .3f}}, and no form on the recorded glomeruli gives that room.
  - **If the boutons keep an equal weight,** the megamat stage error keeps, at its largest stage, a part that no form found so far lowers, and that may be a difference between sets of recordings. It still orders models there. Two halves of the bouton flies order the saved settings alike, at a consistency of {{json:results/E143_megamat_fly_sampling/splits.json:stages.pn_bouton.median_without_e135 ~ .2f}} at the median split, without the four models whose PN stage was set from the recorded dendrites ([above](#fly-sampling)).
  - **If the boutons weigh less or are left out,** the megamat stage error rests on the dendrites, the claws and the somata, and the claws and the somata do not order models that differ only in the calyx. The verdicts so far do not turn on it: the {{json:results/E142_dendrite_bouton_step/classes_step0.json:len(reclasses.equal_weight) ~ w}} classes of the fits and of the settings along the normalization that were read again with the boutons left out are all as scored ([above](#dendrite-bouton-step)).
  - **The second decision: the yardstick for a stage recorded in other flies.** Each stage is read against its own flies, whose floor at the boutons is {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_stage_table.csv:group=megamat_fitted;stage=pn_bouton:strength_floor ~ .2f}}. The model's input is the ORN recordings, and the boutons, the claws and the megamat somata were recorded in other flies by another experimenter. Two sets of flies read at one stage, the megamat somata and the control flies' somata, are {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=megamat_in_control_units:measured ~ .2f}} and {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=control_in_megamat_units:measured ~ .2f}} one-fly units apart, where their floors are {{E146/identities.csv:name=control_floor:reproduced ~ .2f}} and {{E146/identities.csv:name=megamat_floor:reproduced ~ .2f}}.
  - **If each stage stays held to its own flies,** the room below a model at those stages may hold a difference between sets that no model can close. Fits and gates would go on counting it as error.
  - **If a stage is held to what two sets of flies differ by,** the boutons show little room. The fitted model's boutons lie inside both intervals between the two sets of somata, {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=megamat_in_control_units:lo ~ .2f}} to {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=megamat_in_control_units:hi ~ .2f}} and {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=control_in_megamat_units:lo ~ .2f}} to {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=control_in_megamat_units:hi ~ .2f}} over resampled flies. That yardstick would rest on the one pair of sets that exists, at the somata and not at the boutons, with {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=megamat_in_control_units:n_scored ~ w}} flies in one set.
  - **What could be read first, with no new recordings.** The megamat stage error without the boutons, on every split of the flies, from saved runs: whether it still orders the saved settings alike has not been read. And two sets by one experimenter: the older megamat soma recordings, held back at present, against the {{E146/sets.csv:comparison=megamat_in_control_units:n_scored ~ w}} in use. That would say how much of a difference between two sets is the experimenter's, and it needs the word of those who direct the project.
  - **What would separate a step from a difference between sets.** The dendrites and the boutons recorded in the same flies. Those would be new recordings.
- **A step from the ORNs to the PN dendrites in the form that the flies show (a lead, bounded and not reopened).** The global normalization gains before the calyx by spreading the odors' strengths, and it leaves a dependence on the summed input that the flies' step does not show ([above](#normalization-ladder)). The candidate was a step with terms per glomerulus, held to the recorded dendrites.
  - **What bounds it.** The recorded dendrites themselves, the most that such a step can reach on the recorded glomeruli, have been put under the fitted model's calyx: the dendrites come nearer the flies, and the boutons and the claws move away ([above](#dendrite-bouton-step)).
  - **What is closed.** Such a step as a way to bring the stages after the dendrites nearer the flies, and with it the prospect of keeping the fitted model's gain there without the weak global term. That gain stays tied to a term that the flies' step does not show.
  - **What is left.** The step can still be judged on the dendrites as a stage of its own. The bound does not cover the {{diff:json:results/E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/timing_step1.json:size.n_input -- json:results/E135_antennal_lobe_dendrites/lock.json:len(glomeruli) ~ w}} input glomeruli that the dendrite recordings do not hold, and no recording holds them.
  - **What was to reopen it has not been found.** It was to be taken up again with a step from the dendrites to the boutons that re-orders the odors. Neither one weight per recorded glomerulus nor a feature of the whole odor makes that step on odors that it was not fitted to ([above](#bouton-step-forms)). Without such a step, a dendrite stage nearer the flies hands the boutons the flies' spread on the wrong order.
  - **What could still reopen it.** The decision on the yardstick (the entry above). Under the recorded dendrites the boutons and the claws moved away from the flies by {{json:results/E142_dendrite_bouton_step/step1_classes.json:classes.dendrites_carry.stages.pn_bouton.change ~ +.3f}} and {{json:results/E142_dendrite_bouton_step/step1_classes.json:classes.dendrites_carry.stages.kc_claw.change ~ +.3f}}. If the dendrites and the stages after them are read as two sets of recordings that differ, part of such a rise is what a model that follows the first set would show against the second. It would then not count against a dendrite stage nearer the flies by itself. How much of the rise a difference between sets could account for has not been read.
  - **As the rules stand it stays closed.** No stage is bought with another, and a step that brings the dendrites nearer the flies raises the boutons and the claws.
- **The claw and APL forms at the fitted antennal lobe (read in part; a lead for the rest).** APL's inhibition of the claws as a gain and the per-claw threshold were tried on the family's PN stage, where neither gained more than a control fit.
  - **The gain has been set, not fitted, at the weaker normalization.** With the family's scale of the inhibition among KCs its megamat stage error is within a standard error of the fitted model's, and the somata's other views and validation2 get worse ([above](#kckc-rise)). So the gain does not take the place of the larger inhibition among KCs.
  - **What is not known.** The per-claw threshold has not been read there, and neither form has been fitted there. If one adds on wirings and flies that it was not fitted to, the stages name a second structural choice. If neither does, the forms of the calyx are not what limits the stages. Megamat's claws and somata do not order forms of the calyx at the weaker normalization ([above](#fly-sampling)), so what such a fit adds would have to show on the held-out panels.
- **Whether the APL16 recordings join the fit (a decision for those who direct the project).** The megamat stages and the APL effect pull apart: every setting that gains on the one loses on the other, the weaker normalization and each of the fit's calyx scales alike ([above](#normalization-ladder)). The price grows with the scale of the inhibition among KCs as well ([above](#kckc-rise)). A fit with both in its loss would say whether one model can hold both. The APL16 panel would then no longer be a held-out panel.
- **The reading of the mixture sessions (open).** Against the components' second block the fitted model is further from the flies than the family, and against their first block it is not. If the second-block reading were taken into the test, the fitted model's standing on the held-out panels would have to be read again ([caveats](#caveats)).
- **aa between the claws and the somata (read with no simulation; what remains open).** In the flies aa rises from the claws to the somata above what its input predicts, through KCs that respond to few odors. No saved run of the model does this ([above](#claw-soma-aa)). aa's entry is also much of why two halves of the megamat soma flies order the saved settings differently ([above](#fly-sampling)): a lead, as any single odor's entry is.
  - **A mechanism that lifts aa (a lead, not yet a proposal).** It would be a mechanism of the calyx or of the KC, with no term for aa, judged on wirings and flies that it was not fitted to. If it raises aa, the megamat stage error has room down to the error without aa's entry. With the weaker normalization and the family's calyx that room is larger: the other odors come nearer the flies at the somata there, and aa moves away ([above](#normalization-ladder)). If none does, the cause lies in aa's input.
  - **aa's input as each ORN fly recorded it (planned, and not run).** The runs were to be made only if the flies' claws carried aa in few strong regions in {{json:results/E140_aa_claw_soma_step/lock.json:classes.i.flies_min ~ w}} of the {{json:results/E140_aa_claw_soma_step/lock.json:classes.i.flies_n ~ w}} claw recordings, and they did in {{json:results/E140_aa_claw_soma_step/classes.json:i.flies_count ~ w}}. Whether that is close enough is a decision for those who direct the project. If one fly's aa brings the model's somata into the soma flies' range, the answer is inside what the ORN flies showed. If none does, aa's input as recorded does not hold it.
  - **aa's input through the glomeruli fed by ORNs with ionotropic receptors, raised (held for a decision of those who direct the project).** It sets an input factor by the KC recordings. The reading adds a caution: the model's aa responders already draw more of their claws from those glomeruli than any other odor's, and they are broadly tuned. So a stronger input there may raise aa's response rate without making its responders selective.
- **The glomerulus that carries PEA's input (a lead for validation2).** The model's input does not hold it. Ten other glomeruli were added from measured responses in the earlier work (the measured input for the glomeruli without ORN input), and whether this one can be is open. The lead comes from a reading of validation2, so a gain there would need other held-out odors or flies.
- **Held and not run: the megamat odors' input searched for ρ on odor-odor distances.** It searches many input factors, and such searches are set aside. The other open proposals bear on the APL effect and are described in the APL16 Summary.

## Footnotes {#footnotes}

### How one-fly units and the floor are computed {#fn-one-fly}

Each view has one or two parts. Strength by odor has the odors' response rates and the odors' mean responses. Sharing between odors has the cosine distance on which units respond and on how strongly the responders respond. Breadth among units has one part, and so has the glomerulus view.

- **A part's one-fly unit** is the root mean squared distance of one fly's part from the mean part of the other flies, over the flies and the part's entries.
- **A part's error** for a model is the root mean squared difference between the model's part and the flies' mean part, over the entries, divided by that unit.
- **A view's error** is the root mean square of its parts' errors, and a population error is the root mean square of its views' errors.
- **The floor** is the mean error of {{const:kcq/population_fit.py:N_BOOT ~ ,d}} sets of the same flies, drawn with replacement, each scored as a model is against the measured set.
- **One fly against the others** is each fly's error against the mean of the other flies, in the units of all the flies.

A unit from four flies is itself uncertain. In the best family fitted to all megamat stages at once ([above](#stage-fit)) a half of a stage's flies can be two flies. There the one-fly units of all the stage's flies are kept, since two flies give one difference as their unit.

### The monotone curve per stage, and the two readings of a stage {#fn-stage-curve}

A calcium indicator's signal is some rising function of a unit's activity, and that function is not known. So a model's values at a stage are not compared with the flies' as they are.

- **The curve.** The model's pooled values, over every unit and odor of the stage, are mapped onto the flies' pooled values rank for rank, at {{json:results/E134_population_baseline/lock.json:readings.constants.N_CURVE_KNOTS}} evenly spaced quantiles. The curve is non-decreasing and is set for each wiring, with APL on.
- **What it cannot do.** It is set on two pooled distributions, with no odor, unit or pair label. A permutation of either side gives the same curve, so it cannot move an odor's strength relative to another's except through the values themselves.
- **The noise.** After the curve the model's values get the flies' trial noise: the spread of single trials about their odor means, averaged over as many trials as the flies were given.
- **The criterion.** A model unit then responds if its value is above the flies' criterion of the stage.

Every stage has a second reading fixed beforehand: a straight line in place of the curve, another pooling of the boutons or the claws, or at the KC somata raw spike counts with two or more spikes as a response. A stage whose class differs between its two readings is "not readable".

### The response criterion at each stage {#fn-criteria}

A response of the flies is counted in units of noise, so that an indicator's scale does not enter.

- **At the ORNs and the PN dendrites** the unit is the spread of single trials about their odor means, and a glomerulus responds above {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=orn__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:criterion ~ .0f}} of those units.
- **At the boutons and the claws** the unit is the noise before the odor, the standard deviation of the region's signal within the pre-odor frames, and a region responds above {{E134_population_baseline/flies_views.csv:population=pn_bouton__megamat__wild_type;name=strength:criterion ~ .0f}} of those units.
- **At the KC somata** the unit is the same, and the level is set per set of recordings. For the APL16 panel it is {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_soma_criteria.csv:cohort=apl16, control flies:criterion ~ .2f}}, the level at which the control flies keep the response rate of their experimenter's own criterion, {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_soma_criteria.csv:cohort=apl16, control flies:rate_mean ~ .3f}}.
- **The megamat and validation2 somata** take {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_soma_criteria.csv:cohort=megamat, Remy's four flies:criterion ~ .2f}}, the level at which the megamat soma flies keep the response rate of their experimenter's own criterion, {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_soma_criteria.csv:cohort=megamat, Remy's four flies:rate_mean ~ .4f}}. At the APL16 panel's level they would respond at {{E134_population_baseline/writeup_soma_criteria.csv:cohort=megamat, Remy's four flies:rate_mean_at_apl16_criterion ~ .3f}}.
- **The mixture somata as uploaded** are in each cell's z-score over its session, with a level set the same way. The readings by position in the session are in baseline-noise units.

A model's KC responds on raw spike counts if it fires two or more spikes to the odor.

### The realism bands {#fn-realism-bands}

A model run on the APL16 panel is flagged if it leaves one of four ranges. The model's side is counted on raw spike counts, two or more spikes as a response, and the flies' side in baseline-noise units.

- **The response rate with APL on** is between {{const:kcq/apl_effect.py:RATE_ON_BAND.0 ~ .3f}} and {{const:kcq/apl_effect.py:RATE_ON_BAND.1 ~ .3f}}. The control flies' is {{data:noise_ctrl_frac ~ .3f}}.
- **The ratio of the response rates with APL off and with APL on** is between {{const:kcq/apl_effect.py:RATE_RATIO_BAND.0 ~ .1f}} and {{const:kcq/apl_effect.py:RATE_RATIO_BAND.1 ~ .1f}}. The flies' is {{data:noise_rate_ratio_fraction ~ .2f}}.
- **The response rate ρ with APL on,** the correlation over odors between the model's and the flies' response rates, is at least {{const:kcq/apl_effect.py:RATE_RHO_MIN ~ .2f}}.
- **The distribution of the number of odors per KC** is no further from the flies' mean distribution than the most atypical recording is from the others: {{data:noise_ctrl_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .3f}} with APL on and {{data:noise_tnt_odors_per_kc_tv_max ~ .3f}} with APL off, in total variation distance.

The bands are reported and never optimized. On megamat the overall response rate is an operating point: it is held at the megamat flies' value and reported beside it.

### The APL-effect statistics {#fn-apl-effect}

For each of the 120 odor pairs of the APL16 panel, the per-pair APL effect is the distance between the two odors' KC responses with APL on minus their distance with APL off. In the flies that is the control flies' distance minus the distance with APL output blocked (TNT), each the mean over the recordings of the cosine distance. In a model it is taken from one run with APL on and one with APL's output weights at zero.

- **The APL-effect loss** is the root mean squared difference between the model's and the flies' per-pair effect. Its floor is the loss of a resampled set of the recordings against the measured set.
- **The mean APL effect** is the mean over the pairs, and **the pattern part** is the loss with both means removed.
- **The pattern part splits exactly** into an amplitude part, how much wider or narrower the model spreads its effect over the pairs than the flies do, and a shape part, which is zero when the model's effect is the flies' stretched about its mean.
- **The two correlations** over the pairs, ρ and Pearson r, are descriptions. No model is chosen by a correlation: neither sees the mean or the spread of the effect.

On this page the statistics are read through the calibrated readout, a saturating function of a model KC's spike count whose half-saturation is set on the model's own counts with APL on. The fits that made the family, the APL-effect fits and the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings, and every other reading of these statistics are on the page "APL16 Summary".

### Odor concentrations in each dataset {#fn-concentrations}

The megamat panel is at 10^-3 at every stage, in the recordings and in the model's input. The mixtures' ORN input is the recorded response to each mixture, not a sum or a maximum over its components.

The 16 odors of the APL16 panel and the ORN recordings that stand in for them in the model (log10 of the dilution by volume). Twelve odors are at the megamat panel's concentration. For the four odors shared with validation2, the KC recordings used other concentrations than the ORN recordings of that panel.

| odor | KC recordings of the APL16 panel | ORN recordings used | ORN panel | difference (ORN minus KC, log10 units) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2h, IaA, pa, 2-but, eb, ep, 6al, t2h, 1-8ol, 1-5ol, 1-6ol, ms | -3 | -3 | megamat | 0 |
| paa | -3 | -4 | validation2 | -1 (ORN weaker) |
| -aPine | -2 | -1.5 | validation2 | +0.5 (ORN stronger) |
| euc | -2 | -1.75 | validation2 | +0.25 (ORN stronger) |
| B-myr | -2 | -2 | validation2 | 0 |

**The substitution rule.** Where no ORN recording exists at the concentration that the KCs were given, a recording within one log10 unit, inclusive, stands in. Every result that involves the substituted odors' input is then approximate: here everything of the APL16 panel that involves paa, euc or -aPine. The validation2 panel is read at matched concentrations on both sides.
